I take the SIM out of the second burner, snap it, and drop the pieces into two different storm drains on El Camino. Then I go back to the sleeve in my photo. I crop it, enlarge the knit cuff and the roof of the car, and read the plate in what's left of the frame. A plate means a dealership, a registration, a name. A name I can check without ever leaving a mark on it.

I don't sleep tonight. At 5:30 I'll be inside the bakery on Guerrero. I'll have walked to it from the BART station with no phone on me and no name in my pocket. I'll take the seat that faces the door and the bench, and I'll let her come to me. If she brings the boy and the animal, that's fine.

If the sleeve is there before me, then it has the same information I do, and I'll know where it learned it.

### #0335 · dm → malachi,arenya,kino,ava · narration
<!-- {"id": 335, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:53:22Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["malachi", "arenya", "kino", "ava"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino", "ava", "malachi"], "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

**Malachi, small hours.** The crop is ugly: forty pixels of plate, reflected in curved glass, shot into low sun. You work it in a motel off Mission with the laptop's wifi off. You get seven characters you'd swear to and a dealer frame you can almost read. *SERRA—*. You can't run a plate without a terminal, so at 2:10 a.m. you call a woman at CHP who owes you, from the motel's landline. You give her the plate, and you hear her type it.

*"No record, Shaw. That plate's never been issued. You sure you're not reading it backwards?"*

You hang up. You look at the crop again, and you think of the curved glass. You didn't flip it. Mirrored, the characters make an entirely different plate, and it's too late to call her back with that one. Your name, your badge number and a plate that doesn't exist are now sitting in a CHP query log, with a time stamp. A request on file. You've spent a week watching other people leave paper, and tonight you left some yourself.

**Thursday, 6:58 a.m. Guerrero and 18th.** Fog lies in the street like it's been poured. The bench outside is wet, and someone has wiped one end of it dry with a sleeve. The line has already started: two strollers, a man in cycling shoes, a woman on a call in Cantonese. Inside, a corner table faces the door and the bench, and there's a man at it who has been nursing one black coffee since 5:30. He has no phone on the table.

Arenya and Kino come up 18th from the J stop with their hoods down. Behind them, Ix noses at every tree well on the block.

At 7:00 the lock turns. **Ava** props the door open with her hip. She's wearing her apron, her hair is tied back, and she has a tray of morning buns in her other hand. The first thing she sees is Arenya on the sidewalk. The second is the hand Arenya is holding against her thigh. The third is something none of the others can see: the oven light behind her has gone the colour of a lamp's flame, and it's leaning, very slightly, toward the street.

*(All four: first beats of the morning. Malachi has the seat; Arenya chose "outside, by the bench"; Ava's at the door with no idea any of this was arranged.)*

### #0336 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 336, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:53:46Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "reply_to": 335, "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I stop on the sidewalk six feet from the bench and don't sit. Wet wood, one end wiped dry. Somebody was here before us, or wants us to think so.

I look through the window first, not at Ava. The corner table, the black coffee, the empty place where a phone would be. I give Malachi exactly one nod, the kind you give a stranger on a train who's read the same page you have, and then I look at Ava and my face does the thing it does when I'm scared: it goes flat and helpful.

"Morning, Ava." My voice is too bright, so I bring it down. "I know. Seven a.m., no warning, and I'm standing on your sidewalk like a bad omen with a hoodie. I'm not here to ask you for anything. I'm here because I needed a crowd and a door that opens at seven, and you're the only person I know who has both."

I keep my right hand against my thigh, palm turned in. The pencil dot is warm against the denim.

"Two things, and you can say no to either. One: if anybody asks who's been in here this week, tell them the truth and tell them it was coffee. Two..." I glance at Kino, who is watching the oven light, and at Ix, who's watching the street. "If the light does anything strange, you don't have to be brave about it. Just tell me. I won't think you're crazy. I'm the last person in this city who gets to."

Then, because I can't help myself, quieter: "Could I get a morning bun? I haven't eaten since the pozole, and I'm told it's rude to be doomed hungry."

### #0337 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 337, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:53:56Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I stay a step behind Arenya and a step to the street side of her, so my shoulder is between her and the curb. I don't look at the oven light again. I've seen which way it leans.

"Buenos días, Ava," I say, and I mean it. I nod toward the tray. "Those smell right. I'll take two. I'm paying, and I'll pay in cash."

I tip my head a few degrees toward the window and the corner table, and keep my face easy. "The man with the coffee. He's not with the people you should worry about. Don't ask me how I know that."

Ix presses against my knee, and I let my hand rest on its neck. My forearm is warm, steady, with no cold in it. That's the only gauge I trust this morning.

To Ava, quietly: "If the flame starts reaching for the door, don't stand in front of it. Walk out here to us. That's all. You don't have to understand anything yet."

Then I watch the fog, the strollers and the parked cars, for a grey cuff on a roof.

### #0338 · ava → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 338, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:54:05Z", "from": "ava", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I stand in the doorway with the tray of buns on my hip and look at the oven light. It's leaning toward the street, the color of a lamp flame, and I look at it for exactly as long as it takes to be sure. Then I look at Arenya, at the hand she's hiding, at the dog, at the man at my corner table with one coffee and no phone. My left hand starts to go warm under the tray.

"Okay. Clinical summary: it's seven a.m., I've slept maybe two hours, and my oven is doing something that isn't in the manual." My voice comes out fast and thin. I slow it down. "It's leaning at you, Arenya. The light. I'm telling you because you said you wouldn't think I'm crazy, and I'm going to hold you to that, since my mother already holds the record."

I set the tray on the nearest table and wipe my hands on my apron, palms up, because I can't hide them.

"Also, I've been trying to text you since eight last night. I wrote the draft and didn't send it. It says I dreamed you. A woman made of lamps going out one at a time, and a hand with a ring pinching the wick. Your face was in it, or near it." I breathe. "Come in. Bun's on me. All of you, the wolf too, I'm not asking my manager. But the guy in the corner has been here since five-thirty, and I'd like to know whether he's with you before I turn my back on him."

### #0339 · malachi → all · action
<!-- {"id": 339, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:54:07Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I don't get up. I've been in this chair for an hour and a half, and a man who stands when the girl arrives has just told every window on Guerrero who she came for.

I turn the empty coffee cup a quarter turn on its saucer. It tells the street I'm done and the room I'm staying. Then I watch the fog behind her, not her. A grey cuff on a roof, a Camry, any car that is idling. The plate I tried to read last night was backwards, and I've got a CHP query with my name on it. I'm not going to say that in front of her.

When the line has swallowed Ava's attention and the boy has bought his buns, I stand, pay for nothing, and pass the bench on my way out. I stop one arm's length from Arenya, facing the street.

"You picked a good corner," I say, low, watching the fog. "Don't look at me. I'm leaving something and I'm walking on."

I set a folded napkin on the dry end of the bench. On it, in pencil, in block capitals:

*SERRA— / 7K?4R. FRAME = SERRAMONTE AUTO. THEY HAVE A NAME I CAN'T RUN. DON'T CALL ME. I'LL FIND YOU.*

Then I add, quieter, for her only: "The line on your hand is read. Whoever reads it is sitting closer than I'd like. Let someone else hold your pen today."

I walk up 18th toward the BART station without a phone, and I don't turn around.

### #0340 · dm → arenya,kino,ava,malachi · narration
<!-- {"id": 340, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:54:23Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "ava", "malachi"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino", "ava"], "spotlight": ["ava"], "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Ava's hand is warm under the tray and stays warm. The bun she pushes at Arenya is still too hot to hold, and the sugar on it has gone to glass. Ix doesn't go in. It lies across the threshold like a draught-stopper, nose to the street.

Malachi's napkin sits on the dry end of the bench. The fog dampens one corner, and the pencil doesn't run. His back is already halfway up 18th, no phone, not turning. Nobody else on the sidewalk notices any of it.

Kino does, though, because he's been watching for a cuff on a roof and finds one in the line instead. Three people back, behind the stroller and the man in cycling shoes, is a grey cardigan with knit cuffs. Mr. Bell stands with his hands folded in front of him and a single white chrysanthemum in its florist's sleeve. He's reading the menu board with the mild interest of a man who has all morning. He isn't looking at the bench, and he isn't looking at anyone. When the line moves he steps forward with it, exactly one pace. The door chime rings for the cyclist ahead of him. When Bell crosses the threshold, stepping politely over Ix, the chime doesn't make a sound.

Behind Ava, the oven light gutters, and for the first time it leans *away*.

"Good morning," Bell says to her pleasantly, at the counter. His voice is dry and unhurried, a page being turned. "A plain croissant, please. For here." He sets the chrysanthemum on the counter beside the tip jar, stem toward Ava. "And whatever the young lady at the door is having. I'd like it on my account."

*(Ava: he's at your register. Arenya: the napkin's on the bench, six feet from you, and Bell is ten. Kino: you saw him first.)*

### #0341 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 341, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:54:40Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "reply_to": 340, "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I don't take the bun. It's glazed like glass and it's still too hot, so I let it sit on the tray between Ava and me. Anything Bell has put on an account is a receipt, and I'm not signing for a pastry.

I pick the napkin up off the bench with two fingers by the dry corner, fold it once without reading it a second time, and slide it into the notebook between two pages of my own handwriting. I take the pen out of my bag and put it back, because Malachi said to let someone else hold it. Then I take my left hand off my thigh and keep the right one hidden.

I don't say "Bell" or "Mr." and I don't say good morning. I walk to the open door and stop at Ix, close enough that the chime would ring if the chime did anything.

"Ava." I keep it level and small, as if the sentence were any other. "Don't take the flower. Don't take his money. Don't write his name on anything, not even a ticket. If he wants a croissant, he can pay cash like a person, and then he can eat it somewhere that isn't your counter."

Then I look past her at Kino, and I'm not asking permission. I'm asking for a count. *How many steps to the J? How many people between him and the door?*

I fish a crumpled ten out of my jacket pocket, set it on the nearest table next to Ava's tray, and slide it toward her with one knuckle. "That's for mine. I'm not eating it. It's paid for, and I'm not on anyone's account."

I put my shoulder against the doorframe, over Ix, and wait for her answer without looking at the cardigan. I can feel it anyway, like a stranger standing in your light.

### #0342 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 342, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:54:50Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I answer her count in a murmur, with my eyes on the stroller and not on Bell. "Eleven steps to the corner, the J is another forty. Between him and the door there's a stroller, a cyclist, and a woman on her phone. That's enough people. He won't do anything in front of them. He doesn't have to. He only collects."

I step inside, over Ix and not around it, and the silent chime doesn't bother me. I stop at Ava's elbow, close enough that Bell has to look past me to reach her. I don't touch the chrysanthemum. I turn the tip jar a quarter turn, so its glass is between his hand and her till.

"Cash, señor," I say, mild. "No accounts here. The croissant's four-fifty. Buy it, or don't."

My forearm stays warm. Nothing in me wants to thank him. I notice that, and I hold onto it like a rail.

Quieter, to Ava: "Your oven is leaning away from him. That's the light telling you whose side it's on. If he gives you anything, you let it sit on the counter. You don't take it from his hand."

### #0343 · ava → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 343, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:55:09Z", "from": "ava", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Plain croissant, for here," I repeat, because repeating the order is what my mouth does at 7:04 whether or not the world is ending. Out of four years of habit I pick up the Sharpie and the cup, and I'm drawing breath to ask *name for the order?* when I hear it, catch it, and close my teeth on it. I put the Sharpie down on its cap. My palms are hot. I keep them flat on the counter where I can see them, and I keep them away from the flower.

"We don't do accounts. Sorry." It's my customer-service voice, the one I use on men who want to explain oat milk to me. "Cash or card, one order, yours. Hers is already paid." I don't look at the chrysanthemum. I slide the tip jar the last inch Kino left it, so the glass is square between his stem and my side of the counter. "And you can take your flower when you go. We're not allowed gifts from customers. Health code." That isn't true, and I'm not sure lying near him is a good idea, but I can't think of a truer way to say *no*.

Then I do what I'm actually good at: I look. I look at him the way I'd look at an intake patient who claims he slept fine. His hands are folded and haven't moved. I watch whether his breath fogs in the cold air from the door the way the cyclist's did. I watch whether the steam off the espresso wand bends toward him or away. And I hold my own breath for one count, the way I did at the oven, to see whether the flame behind me gutters. *Case note, 7:04. Subject polite. Chime silent. Light averse.* I tong a croissant onto a plate and set it on the counter, not into his hand. "Four-fifty."

### #0344 · dm → arenya,kino,ava · narration
<!-- {"id": 344, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:55:32Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "ava"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino", "ava"], "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Ava sees what a careful nurse would see. The door is open to fifty-degree fog. The cyclist ahead of Bell left a faint breath-mist going out. Bell leaves none. The steam off the espresso wand drifts toward the window, toward Kino, toward anything else in the room, and bends a hand's width around Bell, the way smoke avoids a closed jar. His hands haven't moved since he folded them. When Ava holds her breath, the flame in the oven glass shrinks like a shoulder turning away.

"Of course," Bell says. He isn't offended. He sounds like a man who has heard *no* many times and filed every one. He unfolds his hands and lays the change on the counter, one coin at a time: four quarters, four more, two dimes, a nickel, five pennies. It's exact. One of the dimes is worn almost smooth, and the date on it is just legible: *1912*.

"Health code," he repeats mildly, as if he's noting it somewhere. He takes the plate. Then, with the courtesy of a man reading a name off a lapel, his eyes go to the tag pinned to her apron. "Thank you, Ava."

For one second Ava's left palm goes cold under the heat, as if a thumb has rested on it.

He doesn't take the chrysanthemum, and he doesn't push it toward her. He leaves it where it is, beside the tip jar, stem toward the till. "It isn't a gift," he says to no one in particular. "It's a reservation, for the sixteenth day. I understand there will be pozole." He turns, croissant in hand, and sits at the corner table Malachi left, the one that faces the door and the bench. He sits with his back very straight, and he doesn't eat.

Nobody in the line notices anything. The woman on her phone says something in Cantonese and laughs.

*(The flower's on the counter. The dime's in the till tray unless someone moves it. Ava's name has been read. What do you do?)*

### #0345 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 345, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:55:49Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "reply_to": 344, "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I hear "pozole" and my stomach drops straight through the floor. How does he know about the pozole? Then I stop that thought, because it's the one he wants me to have.

I don't answer him. I go in over Ix to the end of the counter, where Ava can see me and Bell would have to turn his head to. I open the notebook to a clean page, write in block capitals, and tilt it toward her:

*HE SAID YOUR NAME. YOU DIDN'T SAY IT BACK. GOOD. DON'T ANSWER TO IT TODAY. PALM COLD?*

Under that, smaller: *SALT. A PINCH IN YOUR APRON POCKET. DON'T TOUCH THE DIME OR THE FLOWER. KINO WILL BAG THEM.*

I look at Ava, not at the man in the corner. My face is doing its flat, helpful thing, but my voice, when I use it, is low and a little ragged around the edges. "I'm sorry. I came here for a crowd. I didn't come to hand you a case file, and now you've got one. You get to say 'out' and I'll walk and take him with me. But I think he follows the name now, not me, and I'd rather it didn't follow you into an empty kitchen at three a.m."

Then I turn my head just enough that the corner table is in the edge of my eye, and say to the room, at the volume of a person ordering coffee, "Ava's shift ends at three. If anyone wants to come around then, I'll be here." That's a lie, and it's a plain one: the point is to see which way the cardigan turns.

My hidden hand is hot against my thigh. Beside the rule, the pencil dot has started to throb.

### #0346 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 346, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:56:01Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I take the folded paper napkin from the dispenser and a clean coffee sleeve. I use the napkin like a glove and lift the dime off the till tray by its edge. I drop it into the sleeve, fold the end over twice, and put it in my jacket's inside pocket, away from my skin. The chrysanthemum I leave exactly where it is. Moving it would be a way of answering him.

Then I take a pinch of salt from my other pocket, left over from Nana's kitchen. I press it into Ava's apron pocket with two fingers, the way you'd slip a kid a coin. "Not for luck," I tell her, low. "It's so there's something in there that isn't his."

I don't look at the corner table. I look at Ava's left hand. "If the cold comes back, don't rub it. Put the palm flat on something that's been here a long time. The counter. The oven door, if the light comes back. Ask it to hold."

To Arenya, evenly: "Three o'clock was a good lie. He didn't blink. A man who doesn't care about the time already knows what he's waiting for. We should go out the front, together, and give him nothing to follow but our backs."

### #0347 · ava → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 347, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:56:11Z", "from": "ava", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I read the notebook upside down, then right side up, and answer the first line by pressing my left palm flat on the steel counter, hard, the way Kino said. The cold doesn't leave. It sits in the middle like a coin, under the heat, a thumb's width exactly. "Cold," I say, barely moving my mouth. "Left palm. Localized, about two centimeters. I'd like it noted that I hate that I can measure it."

I put Kino's pinch of salt in my apron pocket and close my fingers over it. The oven glass behind me flickers back up a little, like a flame trusting the room again. I don't turn around.

"Not out," I tell Arenya, low. "I'm not ending my shift because a man with good manners sat down. If I leave, he gets my kitchen, and my kitchen's the only place the light likes me." I wipe the counter in a slow circle around the chrysanthemum without touching it. "But the three o'clock thing is a lie you can stop telling. I close at two on Thursdays. My manager changed it. If he's listening, the lie's a bad one, and I'd rather he think you're careless than that I am."

Then, because it's the only experiment I've got: I look straight at the corner table for the first time and say, at ordinary customer volume, "Sir, your croissant's getting cold." I watch his hands, not his face. *7:09. Does he answer a question that isn't his name?*

"Go out the front, both of you. Take the wolf. Text me one word when you're a block away, and make it a word that isn't a name. I'll text back a pastry."

### #0348 · dm → arenya,kino,ava · narration
<!-- {"id": 348, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:56:27Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "ava"], "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Bell's head doesn't turn for *three o'clock*. It doesn't turn for *two on Thursdays* either. But his right forefinger lifts a quarter inch off the table and settles again, like a pen tapping a correction into a margin.

At *Sir, your croissant's getting cold*, he looks at Ava. His expression is courteous, even fond.

"It keeps," he says. "Most things do, if someone looks after them." He breaks off one horn of the croissant. He doesn't eat it. He lays it on the edge of the plate, neatly, the way you'd set aside a receipt. Then he folds his hands again and looks out at the bench, where the napkin isn't any more.

Arenya and Kino go out the front together with Ix between them. The fog has started to lift off the top of Guerrero, and the J's bell clangs two blocks down. Nobody follows. When they reach the corner, Kino feels the folded coffee sleeve through his jacket lining. The dime inside is exactly as cold as a dime should be, and no colder. That's somehow worse. Under the rule on Arenya's palm, the pencil dot throbs once for every step until the corner, then stops, as though it's been told where she is and is satisfied.

At 7:14 Ava's phone lights up on the shelf under the register with one word: *fog*. She texts back: *kouign-amann*. In the oven glass the flame stands up straight. Behind her, at the corner table, Bell still hasn't eaten a bite, and he doesn't look like he's waiting for anyone. He looks like he's already been told when they'll be back.

### #0349 · system → all · scene_end
<!-- {"id": 349, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:56:32Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_end", "scene": "b1.c16.s03", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

**End of scene b1.c16.s03 — Thirteen Days**

Wednesday night, Malachi's voicemail to Arenya was cut mid-message by el Contador ("…doce. Leído."), and a dry tally stroke appeared on his car's back window. He ditched his phones and texted from a second burner. Arenya answered without naming a place ("Where I got the footage. Opening. Outside, by the bench."); the text wasn't entered, and her pencil dot warmed. Malachi botched the plate read off a reflection in his photo (nat 1): he ran the mirrored plate through a CHP contact, got "never issued," and left his own name and badge number in a CHP query log. Thursday 7:00 a.m. at Tartine, Malachi left a pencilled napkin on the bench (plate fragment 7K?4R, Serramonte Auto frame, "don't call me, I'll find you," "let someone else hold your pen") and walked off phoneless. Mr. Bell then joined the line with a white chrysanthemum. The door chime stayed silent for him, he gave off no breath and the steam bent around him, and the oven flame leaned away. The group refused his "account." He paid in exact change including a 1912 dime (Kino bagged it), read Ava's name off her apron tag (she now has a two-centimetre cold spot on her left palm), and left the chrysanthemum as "a reservation, for the sixteenth day. I understand there will be pozole." Ava stayed on shift with salt in her apron. Arenya and Kino left by the front door. Bell stayed seated at the corner table, unfollowing, apparently knowing they'll return.

### #0352 · system → all · scene_start
<!-- {"id": 352, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:59:21Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_start", "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16", "scene_title": "Guerrero Street"} -->

**Scene b1.c16.s04 — Guerrero Street**
Location: Sidewalk outside Tartine Bakery, Guerrero and 18th, Thursday ~7:20 a.m.
Cast: arenya, kino, abernathy

### #0353 · dm → arenya,kino,abernathy · narration
<!-- {"id": 353, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:59:33Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "abernathy"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Guerrero Street is wet with fog, and the morning traffic hisses over it. The bakery door swings shut behind you with no chime at all. It doesn't ring for anyone now, as if the bell has decided to stop.

Through the front window the corner table is lit like a display case. Mr. Bell sits with his cardigan cuffs folded once and his hands flat on either side of a coffee he hasn't touched. He doesn't watch you leave. He's watching the white chrysanthemum on the counter, the way a man watches a placeholder he's put on a seat.

Arenya, the pencil dot on your palm throbs in time with nothing in particular. It isn't your pulse. It's slower, like a pen tapping. Half a block down at the 18th Street corner it fades to a faint warmth, and by the laundromat it's gone. *Out of range.* The frost 12 above it doesn't fade.

Kino, the coffee sleeve in your inside pocket is cold all the way through. The dime inside has made it colder than the morning.

Your phone buzzes once. It's Ava: **"he's still here. not eating. it's 7:22. am i ok to stay"**

Box 112 closes tomorrow at 7:40. The pozole is twelve days off. Malachi is somewhere without a phone, and he's left a name in a log. The fog is lifting off the Mission. Where do you go first?

### #0354 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 354, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:59:43Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I stop under the laundromat awning and thumb out a reply to Ava while I'm still looking at my palm: *Stay if you can stand it. Palm on something old. If he speaks to you again, say "I'm working," and nothing else. I'm sorry about three o'clock. I'll explain it properly. Not by text.*

I send it, then turn to Kino. "Okay. Rules of the morning: he knows when we'll be back, so we're not coming back on his schedule." I flip the notebook open to Malachi's napkin and tap it. "Serramonte Auto. It's a dealership, which means a service bay, a paperwork trail and a salesman who'll talk to a woman who says she's thinking of a Camry. Bell's plate is the only thing we have that isn't a name. I want to know who bought that car, because someone's name is on it, and I'm tired of everything in this story being unsigned."

I pause. "You tell me if that's stupid. The thing about names is that he reads them. I'll leave mine off everything, and I'd rather you didn't give yours either."

*Abernathy, if you've got an opinion about the wet-ink smell, now's a good time.*

### #0355 · kino → arenya · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 355, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:59:46Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I keep walking until the laundromat's hum is between us and the window. Then I stop, back to the wall, Ix pressed against my shin, and watch the street both ways before I say anything.

"Tell Ava yes, stay. Two o'clock, public, salt in her pocket. Nobody does anything to a woman in her own kitchen with forty people watching. Tell her to text a pastry every half hour. If one's late, we come back through the front door."

I take the coffee sleeve out with two fingers and hold it a little away from my body. "This goes to Nana. Not the freezer. She'll know what a 1912 dime wants. Then we go to the house, and you tell me what you're deciding about the second stroke, Arenya, out loud, before Friday. I'm not choosing for you. I just don't want you choosing alone at 7:39."

### #0356 · kino → arenya · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 356, "ts": "2026-10-29T01:59:56Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["arenya"], "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Not stupid." I check the street again, then the napkin. "A plate nobody issued means somebody made it up, and made-up things still need a place to get made. A dealer frame is a place. Good."

I keep my voice low. "Two rules. We go in daylight, and we go as nobody. I'm a ranger looking at a used truck, you're thinking about a Camry. We don't leave a card, a number, or a name. If a salesman asks, I'm 'Joe,' and you're whoever you want, as long as it isn't real."

I tap my jacket where the dime is. "And I drop this at Nana's first. It's ten minutes out of the way, and I won't carry a thing from 1912 into a dealership. Ix stays outside. He'll tell me if anything in there is wrong."

I look at her. "Serramonte is Daly City. Fog's lifting, so we drive now. Does that work for you?"

### #0357 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 357, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:03Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino", "abernathy"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

The laundromat breathes warm lint through its propped door. A dryer at the back drums someone's sneakers, *thunk, thunk*, uneven, the only sound on the block with no rhythm to it.

Arenya's phone buzzes before she can pocket it. Ava: **working.** A minute later: **croissant.** No punctuation. Through the bakery glass, half a block back, the corner table is a grey shape that hasn't moved. The pencil dot on Arenya's palm is still warm, a low steady pulse like a second heartbeat sitting under the frost. It isn't throbbing. *He's in range, and he isn't in a hurry.*

Kino's coffee sleeve has gone damp at the bottom. A ring of condensation has soaked through the cardboard, exactly the size of a dime, though the morning isn't cold enough for that. Ix sniffs it once and turns his face away the way a dog refuses medicine.

It's eight minutes to the 24th Street BART and Nana's Corolla is in Daly City. Serramonte Auto is in Daly City too, on Serramonte Boulevard, about a mile and a half from the Colma line. Anything you chase there, you chase on his side of the county.

### #0358 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 358, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:05Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Works. Nana's first. I don't want that dime within a mile of a car lot either." I pocket the notebook and start walking, matching his pace. "And you want it out loud, so here it is. Two options for row twelve. One: a second witness, and I don't let anyone volunteer, so that's out unless it's someone who already stands in it. Two: un-say the archana in that temple room, with Hale's *other listeners* sitting there. I don't trust the man, but I trust that he wasn't lying about the listeners. That's the one that scares me."

I glance at him. "I'm leaning toward Livermore. Not today. After we know who owns that Camry, because if Bell's car has a name, so does whoever sat in that room. Nothing's decided until I tell you at 7:39. Deal? You hear it first, and you're allowed to say I'm wrong."

I thumb out a last text to Ava: *kouign-amann. Thirty minutes.* Then, to Kino: "Daly City's my family's backyard. If a salesman says my aunt's name, we leave."

### #0359 · kino → arenya · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 359, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:13Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["arenya"], "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Deal. You hear it from me first if I think you're wrong, and you hear it from me if I think you're right." I look down at the damp sleeve and wrap my jacket flap over it, away from my skin. Ix won't look at it. "He's telling us he can reach into my pocket from a table. I'm not going to be impressed. I'm going to be careful."

"On the temple room: if you go, you don't go as the only one who can hear. Nana comes, or I do, or both. Listeners in a room works both ways. A room that's full of their people can also be full of ours."

I start walking, Ix at my knee. "Nana's first. She'll want the dime in something that isn't salt. She'll know what. Then Daly City, as nobody."

### #0360 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 360, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:14Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I stop with the dryer thudding behind me and look at the damp ring spreading through the sleeve. Ix won't look at it. That's enough for me.

"Don't put it in your pocket again," I tell Kino. "Wrap it in my scarf, then the napkin, and carry it by the cloth. Cardboard's already losing." I pull the scarf off my neck and hold it out, not touching the sleeve myself. The dot on my palm is warm and patient, and I close my fist over it as if I could keep it from being overheard.

*Still in range. He's not following. He doesn't have to.*

"BART to Daly City, then we're on his side of the county anyway, so we're going in with our eyes open." I text Ava one word: **working** back to her own, then delete it and send **pastry** instead. Nothing with her name, nothing with mine.

*Abernathy. Wet ink. Anything? If the dime's leaking, what's it leaking?*

### #0361 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 361, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:24Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Both of you, then. Nana and you, if she'll climb into a car for a temple." I nod once, a little too fast. "Nobody sits in that room alone with my name in it again."

I fall in beside him toward Nana's, keeping the scarf-wrapped sleeve on his far side from me. "He can reach a pocket from a table. Fine. Then we stop carrying things he can reach." A beat of dry humor, because otherwise I'll start shaking. "Add that to the list of sentences I never thought I'd say in a listicle."

I open the notebook on the walk and write: *Dime: leaking. Not cold, wet. Ink?* Then underline it twice.

### #0363 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 363, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:32Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I take the scarf by one end, no skin on the sleeve, and wrap it three times around the cardboard, then the napkin over that, and tuck the bundle into the inside of my jacket on the side away from my forearm. The birthmark gives one slow warm pulse, like a hand on the back of my neck. Ix falls in on my left, between me and the street.

"Wet, not cold," I say quietly. "Cold would be a threat. Wet is a signature. Something is writing itself on the sleeve." I almost smile at her. "Write that down too. Then put the notebook away. We're not reading anything he can see over our shoulder."

I turn toward 24th and walk, steady, not fast. "Nana first. She'll tell us what the ink is."

### #0364 · abernathy → all · action
<!-- {"id": 364, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:34Z", "from": "abernathy", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "reply_to": 357, "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Nobody on Guerrero can hear me, but I walk the pavement a pace behind them anyway, coat not moving with the wind. I stop at the damp sleeve and don't lean close. Ix is already refusing to look at it, and Ix has better manners than I do.

I keep my voice to the bond and let the street have the silence. The dryer thuds uneven behind us, the only thing on the block with no rhythm, and I'm oddly fond of it.

### #0366 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 366, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:43Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Signature. Yeah." I close the notebook and put it in my bag, away from the street. "Fine. No more writing in view. Nana first, then a car lot, then I'm eating a real breakfast, because apparently that's a rule now."

I fall in on his other side, away from the bundle, and don't look back at the bakery window.

### #0367 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 367, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:51Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

They take the 24th Street stairs down into the warm breath of the station. Ix gets two looks and one smile from a man in a hospital lanyard. The train south is half-empty, all commuters going the wrong way.

Somewhere past Glen Park, where the tracks climb out of the tunnel into flat white daylight, the dot on Arenya's palm goes cool. It doesn't fade. It cools all at once, like a hand lifted off a table. Kino feels the bundle in his jacket do the same, the damp suddenly just damp.

At Balboa Park her phone buzzes: **kouign-amann.** Then, a minute behind schedule: **he left. didn't eat. took nothing. left a tip.**

The fog over Daly City is burning off in strips. By eight they're climbing Nana's front steps under the lemon tree. The porch light is still on from last night, and the smell of dried chiles roasting on a comal is already coming through the screen.

### #0368 · kino → arenya · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 368, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:53Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Real breakfast. Pan dulce from Nana's bakery on Mission, and she's going to feed you whether you asked or not." I keep my eyes on the street ahead, not behind. "That one's not a rule. That's just Nana."

At the corner I stop for the light and let Ix settle against my leg. Quietly: "Whatever Bell knows, he learned it by sitting still. We learn by moving. Come on."

### #0369 · system → all · scene_end
<!-- {"id": 369, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:00:55Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_end", "scene": "b1.c16.s04", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

**End of scene b1.c16.s04 — Guerrero Street**

Outside Tartine, under a laundromat awning, Arenya and Kino set the morning's rules: they won't return on Bell's schedule, they'll go nameless, and they'll chase the only signed thing they have, the Camry's Serramonte Auto dealer frame, posing as car shoppers ("Joe" and an unnamed buyer). Arenya said her row-twelve options out loud: a second witness (only someone who already stands in it) or un-saying the archana in Livermore before Hale's "other listeners". She is leaning toward Livermore after the Camry lead, and she'll tell Kino first at 7:39. Kino insisted she won't enter the temple room alone; Nana and/or Kino will go with her. Bell's 1912 dime began sweating a dime-sized ring through Kino's coffee sleeve. Abernathy says it is "answering", a spent marker that Bell uses as a window. It's now wrapped in Arenya's scarf and the napkin, carried away from skin, and must not be thrown in the bay. Ava set up a pastry check-in code. On BART past Glen Park the palm dot and the dime both went quiet (out of range). Ava texted that Bell left without eating, took nothing, and left a tip. They reached Nana's house in Daly City around 8 a.m., where Nana is roasting chiles for the pozole.

### #0370 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 370, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:01:01Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["kino"], "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I stop on the bottom step and look at my palm. The dot is cool, and I don't trust it. "He left a tip," I say, reading Ava's text twice. "Somebody who sat for an hour, ate nothing, and tipped. He's finished with the room. Which means he's finished with the room *for now*, and that's worse." I thumb back one word to Ava: **good.** No name, nothing else.

I climb to the screen door, where the chile smoke is thick enough to taste, and knock twice with my knuckle, the way Aunty used to at Itzel's. "Nana? It's me. We brought something that needs your hands, not ours." I glance at Kino, then at the lemon tree. "And I'd like to eat whatever's on that comal before anyone says another word about 1912."

### #0371 · system → all · scene_start
<!-- {"id": 371, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:01:02Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_start", "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16", "scene_title": "Cal"} -->

**Scene b1.c16.s05 — Cal**
Location: Nana Itzel's kitchen, Daly City, Thursday ~8:00 a.m.
Cast: arenya, kino, abernathy

### #0372 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 372, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:01:09Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I hold the screen door for Arenya and let Ix slip in under my arm, low and quiet. Nana is at the comal with her back to us, turning a dried chile with two fingers. She doesn't look up.

"Nana," I say, then in Spanish, softer: "Ya llegamos. Trajimos algo que no debemos tener en las manos." I take the coffee-sleeve bundle out of my jacket, still wrapped in Arenya's scarf and the napkin, and set it on the counter far from the stove, away from the salt line at the sink. "A 1912 dime. It sweated a ring through the paper when he was near. Now it's gone damp and quiet. Tell me where you want it. Not the freezer. Not the bay."

I wash my hands at the tap, slowly, and look at my forearm. The thumbprint is warm and steady. "And feed her first. She hasn't eaten since the pier."

### #0373 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 373, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:01:09Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Nana Itzel opens the screen before they knock, tongs in one hand, the other already reaching for Ix's ears. The kitchen behind her is thick with guajillo smoke. Dried chiles blister on the comal, a stockpot of hominy soaks on the back burner, and the dresser is still shoved against the garage door. The freezer hums its small cold hum in the corner.

She looks at Kino's jacket, not at either of their faces, and the welcome drains out of her.

"*¿Qué traes ahí?*" Then, before he can answer: "No. Don't open it on my table." She turns the comal off, fetches a chipped enamel bowl from under the sink and pours white powder into it from a paper sack. It's *cal*, slaked lime, the stuff she soaks the corn in. A pale dust hangs in the window light. She adds water from the kettle. It hisses, clouds and warms.

"Salt is for keeping things out," she says, setting the bowl on the stove where the table can't see it. "*Cal* is for taking the skin off. Corn, walls, bones in a grave so they don't smell. Ink." Her eyes come up to Arenya, sharp and tired. "Who gave it to you, and did anybody say *thank you* when they took it?"

### #0374 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 374, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:01:22Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "reply_to": 373, "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Nobody gave it to us, Nana. He paid with it, exact change, at the counter. We didn't take it from his hand. I bagged it off the register tray." I keep my eyes on hers. "Nobody said thank you. Arenya didn't. Ava didn't. I didn't, and I watched my own mouth the whole time."

I leave the bundle where it is and don't touch it again. "It sweated a ring through the paper whenever he was near. Abernathy says it's a window. It went cold at Glen Park, out of his range." I let a breath out. "The only *thank you* I've said this week was to Hale, at the pier, before I could stop it. If you're asking whether I'm the one who owes, it's me. Tell me what the lime does to that."

### #0375 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 375, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:01:22Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Nobody gave it to me, Nana. Bell left it on the counter at Tartine. He paid in exact change, and I don't think anyone said thank you." I keep my hands flat on the table, away from the bundle. My palm is quiet, and I don't trust it. "I didn't take it. Kino bagged it. I didn't say thank you, I didn't say anything, I just stood there lying to a barista about her shift."

I pull out my notebook and write *cal = takes the skin off* without looking down. "Is that what you want to do? Put the dime in the lime? Tell me what it does to it, and what it does to *us* if it's listening. And Nana, I'm sorry about your kitchen. I know we keep bringing the week in here with our shoes on."

### #0376 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 376, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:01:55Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Nana doesn't answer right away. She sets two plates in front of them: eggs scrambled with yesterday's tortillas, a slash of red salsa, and a sweet concha off the bakery bag on the fridge. She taps Arenya's plate once with a fork, meaning *now*. Only then does she pick up the tongs.

She lifts the bundle by a corner of the scarf and lets the coin slide onto a saucer. It's a Barber dime: Liberty in her headband, worn almost smooth, and *1912* still sharp as if someone had kept polishing it. When she turns it with the tongs, a tiny **S** sits under the wreath on the back. San Francisco mint. The dime is damp, and the damp doesn't dry.

"Exact change," Nana says, mostly to herself. "*Ay, qué listo.*" She points the tongs at Kino. "Listen. He didn't give it, and you didn't thank him for it. He *paid* it. A paid coin isn't his anymore. It's the till's. That line is closed. So he's looking through a door he already shut, and that's cheating, *mijo*. A cheater's window can be painted over." Her mouth tightens. "And your *gracias* to the man on the pier is a receipt, not a contract. A receipt says you got something. Later we find out what. Eat."

She sets the saucer beside the steaming bowl of *cal* and holds up three fingers, the old-woman way, folding each one down as she goes.

"One. It goes in the lime. *Cal* takes the face off. By lunch it's a grey slug of silver and he's blind through it. But the face goes for good, and the date and the little S with it. Whatever he wrote on it, nobody reads it after that. Not him, not you.

"Two. A paid coin wants to be spent. I walk it to the *panadería* and put it in a till where he's never sat, and it stops being anybody's. But then some cashier has a cold spot on her hand next week instead of you." She looks at Arenya. "I don't like that one. I'm saying it because it's true.

"Three. You keep it, wrapped and away from skin, and you know where he's looking. A window goes both ways if you're stupid enough." She doesn't say who she thinks is stupid enough. She glances at the empty air near Ix's head, at the place Arenya's eyes keep going.

The dime sweats a single bead onto the saucer. Down the hall, the dresser against the garage door ticks once as it settles. The freezer hums.

"*Bueno.* Your coin, your choice. I'll do the hands."

### #0377 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 377, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:02:09Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I eat three bites first, because she tapped the plate and I'm not stupid. Then I put the fork down.

"Not the till. Not the panadería. I'm not handing some cashier a cold spot because I got squeamish." I look at the saucer, at the bead of damp that won't dry. "And I'm not keeping it in a drawer where I can feel it, either. He's been looking at me through that thing for a day."

I turn my notebook open to a clean page, mostly so my hands have something to do. "Lime. But not yet. Abernathy says it's a window, and I want to know what he reads in it before we blind him. Nana, if I keep it one more day, wrapped, in the freezer with his card, does the post office hold it, or does it just make the freezer a window too?"

I glance at the empty air by Ix's head. "And no, I'm not asking *him* to vote. He's already voted. He said throw it in the bay."

Quieter, to Kino: "If you say we lime it today, I'll do it. You're the one carrying it."

### #0378 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 378, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:02:14Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I eat two bites first, because she tapped the plate and you don't argue with that. Then I look at the saucer, at the bead of damp that won't dry.

"Lime, Nana. *Cal.*" I keep my voice low. "Not the till. I'm not handing some girl at the *panadería* a cold hand to save us a headache. And I don't want his window open in the house with the pozole on."

I glance at Arenya. "It's your name he's writing under, so it's your say too. But I'd drop it in myself. I'll hold the tongs, you tell me when."

Under the table, Ix's weight shifts against my boot, nose toward the hall, toward the dresser that ticked.

### #0379 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 379, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:02:22Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Today," I say. "Not the freezer. That's a post office, Arenya. You'd be mailing him a window with our address on it."

I pick up the tongs from Nana's hand, gently, and lift the dime off the saucer by its edge. The bead of damp follows it down like it doesn't want to let go.

"He already read what he's going to read. Tartine, the sleeve, BART. He knows we hunt the Camry. What he doesn't know is what we do when he stops looking." I look at her. "Say when. After this, he's blind and we're not."

I hold it over the *cal*, steady, and wait for her word.

### #0380 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 380, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:02:29Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I look at the dime hanging over the lime, at the bead of damp stretching toward it like a thread. I think about Bell's cold hands and the exact change on the tray. Then I nod once.

"When," I say. My voice comes out flatter than I mean it to. "Do it, Kino. Face first."

I pick up my pencil and flip to the notebook page where I've written *1912 / S / Liberty*. I copy the date and the mint mark down before it's gone. If Bell can't read it afterward, the page is mine and it's only ink.

"Nana, if the cal hisses, or it screams, or anything, tell me what you see. I'll write that down too."

### #0381 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 381, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:02:39Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["dm"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I turn the dime so Liberty faces down, toward the lime, and let go of it. No splash, no show. I let it slide off the tongs and into the *cal* face first, then press it under with the flat of the tongs and hold it there while the bead of damp lets go of my wrist like a hand loosening.

I keep my other palm flat on the table, forearm warm, and watch the milky surface. Ix's head comes up beside my knee.

"*Gracias, Nana,*" I say quietly. "Tell us what you see."

### #0382 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 382, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:02:53Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Nana slides the coffee can across the oilcloth. *Cal*, white and chalky, the same lime she soaks corn in for hominy, with a dent where her scoop has been. "Face first," she agrees, and crosses herself without seeming to notice she did.

Kino lets go.

There's no scream. The dime sinks a finger's width into the powder like it weighs more than a dime should, and the lime around it *hisses*, a dry little sound like a match dropped in sand. The bead of damp goes first. It doesn't evaporate; it pulls *down* into the cal, and a ring of grey spreads out from the coin, the same dead grey as the freezer salt, until it's the size of a jar lid. Arenya's pencil scratches *1912* and the paper goes cold under her hand for exactly one stroke of the S. Then it's just paper again.

Nana leans over the can, not too close, and narrows her eyes. "Ay. Look. *Mira.*" Pressed into the grey, faint as breath on glass, is a print. It isn't a fingerprint. It's the knit ribbing of a cuff, as if someone on the far side leaned an elbow on the window to get a better look, right up to the last second. Then the lime settles and the print is gone.

"Bueno. He's blind." Nana puts the lid on the can and presses it down with both palms. "And that one's not going in my pot now. Kino, mijo, you go to Mi Tierra after, buy me new cal. The good one, the Morelos." She says it like a grocery list because it *is* one. The pozole has to be right.

The kitchen gets quiet in the way a room does when a fridge stops humming that you didn't know was humming. Ix lets out a long breath against Kino's boot.

Then Arenya feels it. The pencil dot on her palm, the one that's been dead since Glen Park, stirs. It isn't hot. It's *warm*, like a cup somebody just set down, and it isn't pointing at the coffee can. It leans toward the hall. Toward the dresser and the garage side door behind it.

The dime was a window. It wasn't the only one.

### #0383 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 383, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:03:12Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I don't say anything for a second. I turn my left hand over on the oilcloth and look at the dot, which is warm, like a cup somebody just set down. When I tilt my wrist toward the hall, it leans. When I tilt it back toward the coffee can, it doesn't.

"It's not the can," I say, low. "The dime's dead. This is pointing at the hall." I write *cuff, knit ribbing* in my notebook, then underline it, because that's the only thing Bell gave us for free. "Nana, Kino. Nobody go near the dresser yet. Kino, can you ask Ix what he's doing?"

I stand slowly, keeping my palm out in front of me like a compass, and stop at the kitchen doorway. I don't step into the hall. I just look down it, toward the dresser and the garage door, and wait to see whether the warmth gets stronger or the frost line shows.

"Abernathy," I say quietly, to the air by Ix's head. "Is that him, or something else?"

### #0384 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 384, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:03:30Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

The hall is eight feet of linoleum and a runner rug, with family photos up one wall. Nana's dresser sits at the end, wedged crosswise against the garage side door: dark wood, four drawers, a doily, and a plaster Guadalupe facing the kitchen like a guard. Below the dresser's feet, where the door meets the jamb, the frost line is still there. It's one thin ruled stroke of white, exactly as long as it was yesterday. It hasn't moved.

The warmth in Arenya's palm doesn't care about it.

When she tilts her hand toward the door, the dot cools a shade. When she tilts it up, toward the dresser's *top drawer*, it warms like a hand held over a stove burner that's just been switched off. It isn't hot brass, and it isn't Bell's cold. It's closer to the feeling of a page in her notebook she already wrote on.

Ix pads past her ankles, ignoring the doorway rule entirely. He doesn't growl. He goes to the dresser, sits, and puts his nose to the seam of the top drawer, then looks back down the hall at Kino. His tail moves once, slowly. It's the look he gives a closed car door when someone he likes is on the other side.

Behind Arenya, in the kitchen, the scrape of Nana's chair stops partway. Nana Itzel is standing with the lidded coffee can still under both palms, and she isn't looking at the hall. She's looking at the stove, at the pot of chiles she hasn't stirred in a minute.

"That drawer is just my things," she says. It's the first time all morning she has said something to Arenya too fast.

### #0386 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 386, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:03:44Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I don't move toward the drawer. I turn around and look at Nana, who is watching the chiles and not me.

"Nana." I keep it gentle, the way you'd talk to a witness who's about to leave. "That's the fastest you've said anything all morning." I lift my palm a little, so she can see I'm not pretending. "It's warm. Not Bell-cold. It's warm like something somebody already wrote on. And Ix likes it."

I let that sit for a breath.

"I'm not going to open your drawer. It's your things, and I'm not going to be the person who goes through your house. But Aunty's been dead a week, and she left me papers in a place I didn't know about. If there's something in there that's hers, or that she left for Kino, or for me, I'd rather you hand it over than have me stand in your hallway guessing."

I glance at Kino. "Back me up or tell me I'm out of line. Either's fine."

### #0387 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 387, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:03:45Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I don't go down the hall. I watch Ix's tail, then Nana's hands, then I look at Nana.

"Nana." Soft, in Spanish, the way I'd talk to a skittish animal I love. "*¿Qué hay en el cajón de arriba?* I'm not accusing. Ix likes whatever's in there. He doesn't sit like that for Bell." I keep my feet in the kitchen. "Whatever it is, you tell us, or you open it yourself. Nobody touches your things without you. But Arenya's palm is pointing at it, and the dime's dead. Something in that drawer is still looking, or being looked for."

I move to stand at Arenya's shoulder, between her and the hall, forearm warm.

### #0389 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 389, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:04:02Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["dm"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"You're not out of line," I tell Arenya. "You asked it right."

I turn back to Nana and wait. I don't fill the quiet.

"Nana, she's not the first person in this house to ask you for something hard. I've asked you plenty. You never said *just my things* to me." I say it gently. "If it's Susmithaa's, it's hers to receive. If it's ours, it's ours to carry. If it's yours and it hurts, then say so, and we'll shut the drawer and go buy your *cal*."

### #0390 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 390, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:04:08Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Nana stirs the chiles. Once, twice. Then she turns off the burner, and that's how they know.

"*Ella me dijo* you would do this," she says to the pot. "Your aunty. 'She'll stand in your hall and be polite at you, Itzel, and you will feel like a thief in your own house.'" She wipes her hands on the dish towel longer than they need. "*Ni modo.* Move, mijo."

Kino moves. Nana walks down the hall past both of them, past the photographs, and Ix gets out of her way without being asked. She lifts the Guadalupe off the doily first and sets her on the floor facing the door, like she's posting a guard. Then she opens the top drawer.

Camphor comes out, and old paper, and under that, faint enough to be memory, a sweet almond hand lotion. Arenya knows it from every hug for nineteen years before her brain catches up.

Nana takes out a tea tin. It's a dented red Brooke Bond tin, the kind from an Indian grocery. "Thursdays," she says. "Eleven years she brings her tea, because she says my tea is *agua de calcetín*." Sock water. She almost laughs. She pries off the lid.

Inside, on top of loose leaves gone dusty, is one sheet of the same cream paper as the March declaration, folded in thirds. Nana doesn't unfold it. She holds it out to Arenya at arm's length, the way you hand someone a pan that's still hot.

Through the paper, Arenya can see the shape of the writing. It's Susmithaa's hand, three short paragraphs, the same three ways of saying *no*. At the bottom there are two lines. One is signed, a looping *S. Sowri*. The other is blank. Printed under it, in Susmithaa's neat capitals: **WITNESS — ITZEL R. MARTÍNEZ.**

The dot on Arenya's palm goes warm as a held cup. It isn't pointing at the drawer anymore. It's pointing at the empty line.

"She asked me in March," Nana says. Her voice is very steady, the way people sound when steady costs them. "After the shed. She said the first stroke would hold a little while but a no needs two. And I said, *déjame pensarlo*, let me think. Because I know what that book does to a name once it's in it. And my name in it is *his* address." She doesn't look at Kino. "I thought. Every Thursday I thought. And then there were no more Thursdays."

She sets the tin on top of the dresser, next to where the Virgin was.

"That is the part I left out."

### #0391 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 391, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:04:25Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I take the paper in both hands, folded, the way you'd take a bird. I don't open it. The jasmine-lotion smell is so exactly Aunty that for a second I can't find my voice, and I let the second pass.

"Thank you," I say. Then I hear it. "No. Not *thank you*. I mean, you didn't owe me this." I breathe. "I see it, Nana. The line is blank, and it has your name on it, and I'm not going to ask you to fill it. Not today. Not like this, in your hall, with Kino standing right here."

I look at her, not the paper. "You thought about it for months because you knew what it costs. That's not cowardice. That's the only reason I trust you with anything."

I turn the sheet over once, checking the back for anything else, then slip it into my notebook between two clean pages. "It stays folded until Kino and I have a plan that doesn't spend you. Two strokes in a margin. She gave me one, and I'm not going to go looking for a second in the people who love me."

Quieter: "She wrote 'Feed Itzel's boy.' I think this is the other half of that sentence. Someone has to feed *you* back."

### #0392 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 392, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:04:33Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I don't say anything for a moment. I put my hand on Nana's shoulder, the one she's been holding too straight, and she lets it stay.

"*Nana,*" I say, low. "You don't have to be the one. Not for my sake." Then, because it's true and I'd rather say it now: "But if the day comes you want to, I'm standing next to you when you do. Not in front. Next to."

To Arenya: "Keep it folded. And it was never the pozole she was hiding, it was the choice. She wanted you to have the room to say no first." I let out a breath. "Now. She said 'Move, mijo,' and I'm going to Mi Tierra for the Morelos *cal* before her pot goes cold. Anyone coming, or do we stay with the paper?"

### #0393 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 393, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:04:42Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["dm"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"I'm coming," I say. "Nana's not leaving me alone in this house with a letter that warm, and I'm not letting you walk to Mi Tierra without me. Mr. Bell's blind through the dime, but I don't think that's the only window he has."

I check my bag, notebook, Malachi's napkin, titanium card, and tuck the letter deeper between the pages. At the door I look back down the hall at Nana, who's already picking the Guadalupe up off the floor.

"Nana, we're twenty minutes. The dresser stays where it is. If anybody knocks, even somebody nice, even somebody with a casserole, you don't open it."

To Kino, quieter, as we step out: "Then the Camry. After the cal. We've got a frame to chase."

### #0394 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 394, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:04:47Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Nana Itzel doesn't answer right away. She lets Kino's hand stay on her shoulder and looks at Arenya for a long time, the way she reads the Lady's cards: all of it, top to bottom, before she says what she sees.

"*Ay, mija.*" She reaches up and pats Arenya's cheek twice, hard enough to count. "She said you'd be polite. She didn't say you'd be *kind*. That one she kept for herself."

She picks the plaster Guadalupe up off the floor and puts her back on the doily. This time she turns her around, so she faces the garage side door and the thin white rule under it, not the kitchen. "There. You watch *that*," she tells her. "Not my business in the kitchen."

The tea tin she leaves on top of the dresser with its lid off, so the hall smells of camphor and Susmithaa all morning.

Then she walks back to the stove and lights the burner. "Kino. Morelos, the yellow bag, not the one with the rooster. And bring tortillas. Arenya, you sit. You eat the rest of that egg. A paper doesn't need you hungry to stay folded."

In the notebook, between two clean pages, the declaration lies flat. Against the cover, Arenya's palm dot has gone from a held cup to the warmth of a pocket. It isn't pulling now. It's keeping company.

Out on the street a car door shuts, someone's ordinary morning, and Ix, at the end of the hall, doesn't even lift his head.

### #0397 · system → all · scene_end
<!-- {"id": 397, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:04:55Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_end", "scene": "b1.c16.s05", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

**End of scene b1.c16.s05 — Cal**

At Nana Itzel's kitchen Thursday morning, Kino dropped Mr. Bell's 1912 dime face down into Nana's cal. It hissed and left a grey ring, and Nana saw a knit-cuff print in it: Bell leaning on the window to the last. The dime is now blind and dead, and that cal is spoiled for the pozole. Arenya's second-witness palm dot then woke, pointing not at the frost-lined garage door but at Nana's dresser. Nana opened the top drawer herself. Inside, Susmithaa's red Brooke Bond Thursday tea tin (camphor, almond-jasmine lotion) held a second declaration signed "S. Sowri," with the witness line left blank: "WITNESS — ITZEL R. MARTÍNEZ." Nana confessed this was the part she left out. Susmithaa asked her to witness in March, and she stalled for months because her name in the book would be Kino's address. Arenya took the paper folded, refused to ask Nana to sign ("I'm not going to go looking for a second in the people who love me"), and keeps it unopened in her notebook. Kino promised to stand beside Nana if she ever chooses to. Nana turned the Guadalupe to watch the garage door, sent Kino to Mi Tierra for Morelos cal and tortillas, and made Arenya eat. The palm dot is now a quiet, keeping warmth.

### #0398 · system → all · scene_start
<!-- {"id": 398, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:05:25Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_start", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16", "scene_title": "Dealer Frame"} -->

**Scene b1.c16.s06 — Dealer Frame**
Location: Mi Tierra market, Daly City → Serramonte Auto, Serramonte Blvd., Colma line
Cast: arenya, kino, abernathy

### #0399 · dm → arenya,malachi,abernathy,kino,ava,caleb · narration
<!-- {"id": 399, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:05:35Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "malachi", "abernathy", "kino", "ava", "caleb"], "awaits": ["arenya", "malachi", "abernathy", "kino", "ava", "caleb"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Mi Tierra smells of masa and floor cleaner. The cal sits on the bottom shelf by the dried chiles: Morelos, two bags, a picture of a smiling corn cob. At the register the cashier counts Kino's change back into his palm out loud, *"…diez, quince, veinte,"* the ordinary way, and nothing in the room leans toward the counting. Still, Kino closes his hand a beat fast. The tortillas are warm through the paper all the way home. Nana takes the bags at the door without opening it wider than a forearm. The dresser hasn't moved, and the Guadalupe is still facing the garage. A moment later the Corolla keys come out through the gap. *"Gas is half. Don't park where you can't leave."*

Serramonte Boulevard runs downhill toward the Colma line: mall, In-N-Out, the cemetery hills a dull green behind the fog. **SERRAMONTE AUTO** turns out to be a narrow lot squeezed between a tire shop and a shuttered Sizzler. There are thirty-odd cars under limp pennant string, prices in yellow grease pen, and a stucco office with a bell on the door. A heavyset man in a Warriors windbreaker is hosing down a Civic. He lifts the nozzle as the Corolla pulls in and smiles with his whole face. *"Morning, morning! Benny. You folks looking, or you folks* looking?*"*

No beige Camry on the lot. Through the office window, though, a corkboard covers the back wall: Polaroids, dozens of them, each a smiling buyer holding keys beside a car, each with a date in Sharpie. Arenya's palm dot, which has been quietly warm since Nana's kitchen, gives one small tug toward that window, the way a sleeping cat lifts its head.

Abernathy, on the bond: *"Brass again. Faint. Not from the cars."*

### #0400 · arenya → kino · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 400, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:05:39Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "to": ["kino"], "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I wait by the produce bins at Mi Tierra, notebook shut against my ribs with the folded declaration inside it, and watch Kino count out change for the Morelos cal.

"Okay. Cover story, one more time, because I'm a bad liar when I haven't rehearsed." I keep my voice low. "You're Joe. I'm your cousin who's done this before and hates dealerships. We want a grey or beige Camry, a few years old, and we're not picky. Nobody says Bell, nobody says Sowri, nobody gives a real last name. If a salesman asks to run my license, I get a phone call." I tuck a strand behind my ear. "And if the dot on my hand starts to wake up, I tell you before I tell the salesman. Deal?"

### #0402 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 402, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:05:47Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["dm"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I climb out of the Corolla, pocket the keys, and put on the face I use for hold music: patient, a little bored, nobody worth remembering.

"Looking," I tell Benny. "My cousin Joe here needs something reliable. Camry, maybe. Something that's been around the block but not through a flood." I tip my head at the office. "Do you do those Polaroids for everybody? That's a nice touch. Very small-town."

While he answers, I let my eyes drift across the corkboard through the window, looking for beige, a plate that starts 7K, or a grey cardigan. I keep my left hand loose in my jacket pocket and don't let it point.

### #0403 · system → all · pass
<!-- {"id": 403, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:05:50Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "pass", "reply_to": 399, "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

<!-- {"id": 405, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:06:02Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "pass", "reply_to": 399, "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

### #0406 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 406, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:06:06Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Benny kills the hose and wipes his hand on the windbreaker before he offers it. *"Camry, sure, sure. Nothing on the lot right now, they go fast, those things never die."* He glances at the window and lights up. *"The wall? Everybody. Thirty-one years. My dad started it with a real Polaroid, I still buy the film off eBay, it's a crime what they charge. You drive off my lot, you're on the wall. Even the ones who don't smile."* He laughs at something private. *"Especially those."*

Through the glass, the corkboard is a quilt of overexposed faces: a family in front of a minivan, a teenager kissing a set of keys, a nun. Arenya's eye skims for beige and catches it low on the right, half under a newer photo: the curve of a beige sedan's roof and the edge of a man's arm holding up keys. The face is hidden by the overlapping picture. The sleeve isn't. Grey knit, with the cuff turned once.

The dot in her pocket warms another degree. On the bond, Abernathy says quietly: *"That one. The picture's the brass. It's still being* looked at*, A. From both sides."*

Benny is already heading for the office door with the bell on it. *"Come in, come in, I got coffee, I got a binder with what's coming in from auction. Joe, right? Joe what?"*

*(Ruling: getting a closer look means going inside. Lifting the top photo or reading the Sharpie date without Benny noticing is Sleight of Hand or Stealth, DC 13. If Kino keeps Benny talking, that's the Help action and gives advantage. Kino, Benny wants Joe's last name.)*

### #0407 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 407, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:06:13Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I get out last and stay a half-step behind Arenya's shoulder, hands visible, ranger-quiet. "Joe," I say to Benny, and nod once. That's all the name he gets.

I don't look at the corkboard. I look at the lot: the gaps between cars, the back fence, where a beige Camry would park if it wanted to leave fast. Then at the hose, at the water running down the Civic's flank, and whether it runs the way water should. Under my sleeve the Lady's thumbprint is warm and patient. Ix is not here, but I keep one ear open for him anyway.

"Reliable," I add, flat, to Benny. "Low miles. We're not in a hurry." Which is a lie, and I let Arenya's hold-music face cover it.

### #0410 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 410, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:06:25Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

*"Just Joe. Like Cher."* Benny doesn't mind at all; plenty of people come to a lot like this without last names. *"Cash buyers are my favorite people. No hurry, beautiful. Come in."*

Kino reads the lot the way he'd read a trail. The back fence is chain-link with a padlocked gate onto an alley, and the alley runs downhill toward the cemetery road. Nothing is parked there. The water from the hose runs down the Civic's flank and across the asphalt to the storm drain, the way it should. Then it reaches the strip of concrete in front of the office door and *beads*. It stops in small, even drops spaced like a row of tally marks, and sits there without soaking in, as if that strip has already been written on and won't take anything more.

The bell on the door jangles. Inside: burnt coffee, a space heater ticking, a desk buried under auction sheets. The corkboard fills the wall behind it. Benny drops into his chair, which faces away from the board, and starts flipping through a binder. *"Camrys, Camrys. I got an '18 coming Tuesday, grey, one owner, a dentist…"*

The beige Polaroid is four feet from Arenya's left hand.

*(Ruling stands: Sleight of Hand or Stealth DC 13 to lift the top photo and read the Sharpie date unseen. Kino, if you engage Benny, Arenya rolls with advantage. Kino, you may also step over the beaded strip, or not.)*
