### #0412 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 412, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:06:35Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Joe's easy, he doesn't like paperwork," I tell Benny, and glance at Kino. "Joe, ask him about the auction binder. Mileage, prior owners, all of it. Make him prove it." Kino has Benny's attention. I wander to the corkboard like a woman admiring a nun.

"Thirty-one years. That's a lot of keys." I say it warmly, and my right hand lifts the corner of the top Polaroid with one fingernail, the way you peel a sticker you mean to put back. I'm looking only at the Sharpie date under the sleeve. I'm not looking at the arm, and my left hand stays in my pocket.

### #0413 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 413, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:06:42Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I step over the beaded strip in front of the door, not on it, and let the bell ring behind me. Then I drop into the customer chair, square to Benny's desk, so his back stays to the wall.

"The dentist's Camry," I say. "One owner. Who's the dentist? Why's he selling? Dentists don't sell a car that's never died." I tap the binder. "Show me the service records, not the auction sheet. Mileage on the sheet and mileage on the dash are two different people."

I keep my voice dull and a little stubborn. A man who doesn't like paperwork but is going to make you read all of it. I keep my eyes on Benny's, and I don't look at the corkboard once.

### #0414 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 414, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:07:09Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Benny takes Kino's challenge like a gift. *"Service records! A man who reads!"* He swivels fully toward the desk and starts digging through a filing drawer that screams on its rails. *"The dentist is a* lady *dentist, Joe, and she's selling because she bought a Tesla and she's going to regret it…"* His back is a wall of Warriors gold, and the drawer is loud enough to cover anything.

The top Polaroid lifts on Arenya's fingernail as easily as a page. Underneath, the beige sedan's photo sits in its own pushpin. The car is new-washed, and on the plate frame a paper dealer tag, SERRAMONTE AUTO, flaps half-loose. The arm in grey knit holds up a key on a cardboard tag. Arenya keeps her eyes off it and on the white strip at the bottom, where Benny's Sharpie has written, in a hand that has written ten thousand of these:

**WALT — 3/19 — 4:51 PM — CASH ✓**

March nineteenth. The same afternoon Hale rented Box 112 with new twenties, fifty-nine minutes after Susmithaa's declaration. Two men, one afternoon, one mile apart.

Something else is there, and only someone looking very carefully at the edges would catch it. The Polaroid's white border is *frosted*. A fine rime runs along the bottom margin, under the Sharpie, as straight as a ruled line. It doesn't melt in the heat from the space heater. In her pocket, the dot goes from warm to *hot*, sudden as a held breath. It doesn't pull toward the photo. It pushes away from it, the way a hand jerks back from a stove.

On the bond, Abernathy is very quiet and very fast: *"Window's lit, love. Not the dime. That. He's lookin' out of his own picture at the room. Not at you, yet; at the* room. *Put the top one back down and it shuts like an eyelid. Take it, and you're carryin' a window in your bag."*

Behind her, Benny slaps a folder on the desk. *"Ha! Oil changes every five thousand, she's a saint."*

*(Arenya: let the top photo fall back, or take the beige one? Taking it unseen needs no roll on that 20. Kino, Benny's yours, and he's talkative. Anything you want to steer him toward?)*

### #0415 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 415, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:07:25Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I read the strip once, slowly, the way you read a license plate you'll have to swear to: WALT, 3/19, 4:51 PM, CASH. Then I let the Polaroid fall back over its twin. It settles flat, and I feel the heat in my pocket drop to warm.

I don't take it. I'm not carrying his window in my bag past the beaded strip and into Kino's car.

Instead I flip my notebook open against my thigh as if I'm doing sums on a payment plan. I write the strip down in my own handwriting: *Walt. 3/19. 4:51. Cash.* Beneath it, smaller: *59 min after S.'s declaration. Hale's twenties, same day.* Then I look up, bored, and say to the room at large, "Dealer frame on a sedan this clean usually means it came off their own lot. Does Serramonte still do trade-ins on the consignment side, or is this a private sale you're brokering, Benny?"

I keep the pen moving, a woman doing math on someone else's money.

### #0416 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 416, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:07:27Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Lady dentist." I lean on the desk, easy, one elbow on the counter so my shoulder blocks his view past me. "Those are the ones who actually change the oil. Benny, the Camry in the Polaroids, the beige one. Who walked in with that? Walt, the board says. He the type who haggles or the type who pays cash and leaves?"

I keep my voice lazy, a guy killing time while his girlfriend looks at pictures. "I ask because last time I bought off a cash guy, the title was a mess. You remember Walt's paperwork? Name on the title, how he signed?"

### #0417 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 417, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:07:48Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

*"Own lot, beautiful. Auction car, came in February, I sold it in March. No consignment, I'm not a charity."* Benny leans back until the chair groans and looks at Kino with fond suspicion. *"Walt. Oh, Walt. You don't forget Walt."*

He tells it the way a man tells a story he's already told at the tire shop. *"Old guy, cardigan in March, nice manners, like a librarian. Doesn't haggle. Doesn't test drive. Pays cash: old bills, soft, every one folded once down the middle, and he counts them onto my desk out loud. In* Spanish. *'Diez, quince, veinte.' Don't speak a word of it otherwise, I tried. Then he signs everything, beautiful hand, fountain pen, and he reads every page twice. Twice! Nobody reads my contracts."* He snorts. *"Title's clean. Walter Bell. Address was a mailbox, Colma Notary over on Mission. Number twelve. Lots of old folks do the mailbox thing."*

He jerks a thumb at the board without looking at it. *"Didn't want his picture. 'I don't photograph, Mr. Benasaraf.' Very polite. I said, Walt, everybody goes on the wall. Took it anyway. Came out funny, see, the face washed right out. Bad film. Forty dollars a pack and it washes out the one guy."*

Arenya's pen is still moving. Kino's elbow is still on the desk. Benny's eyes go from one to the other, friendly, and stay there a beat too long.

*"Funny thing. He called me. Last week, Tuesday maybe. Wanted to know had anybody come asking about his car. I said, Walt, who'd ask about a Camry? He says, 'If they do, Mr. Benasaraf, take their names. I'd like to send a thank-you.'"* Benny pulls a sticky pad toward him and uncaps the Sharpie, all good cheer. *"So. Joe. And the lady is…?"*

### #0418 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 418, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:07:57Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I close the notebook on my pen with a soft, bored snap and give Benny the smile I use on hold music.

"Oh, don't bother with the pad, Benny. We're not buying today. We're window-shopping with a spreadsheet." I stand, hitch my bag onto my shoulder, and keep it between me and the corkboard. "Joe's the one who can't commit. I'm the one who says, 'Let's go look at the Honda place and come back.' You know how it is. Nobody gets a name until there's a number."

I tap the desk twice, friendly, over the sticky pad, so the corner of it lifts away from his Sharpie.

"But that's a great story about Walt. Counting in Spanish. I'd have remembered that." I tilt my head. "If he calls again, tell him the Camry's got a fan. Doesn't need a thank-you note. Joe, babe? Shall we?"

I'm already turning toward the beaded strip, and I let my eyes find Kino's on the way.

### #0419 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 419, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:08:00Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I smile, slow, and slide the sticky pad back across the desk with two fingers. Not rude. Final.

"Joe's fine. Joe Ramos. But you don't need a name for a thank-you, Benny. You need one for a sale." I nod at the board. "Man pays in folded bills, won't be photographed, and calls you to ask who's been asking. That's not a customer, that's a guy checking his fences. I'd want to know why, if it were my lot."

I keep my hands easy on the counter, where he can see them. "Tell you what. We're not here for his Camry. We're here for the dentist's car. Pull that folder again, and give me a number I can't refuse. And if Walt calls back this week, tell him nobody came in but a couple who couldn't afford you."

Under the desk's edge, my forearm is warm. I let it be.

### #0420 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 420, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:08:08Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I push off the desk and put my hand out to Benny. A real handshake, short, dry. "Thanks for the story. Hold the dentist's car till Saturday, we'll call." I don't say which number.

Then I fall in half a step behind Arenya, between her and the corkboard, and walk her through the beaded strip without looking back. Out in the sun I let my breath go. "Colma Notary, mailbox twelve, on Mission," I say, low, in Spanish-flavored English. "Rodel's notary was in Colma too. Either Bell's following the same paper trail, or he laid it." I thumb the cold dime through the scarf in my pocket. "Honda place first, so the lot sees us go. Then we circle back to Mission on foot."

### #0421 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 421, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:08:19Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Box twelve," I say, once the sun is on my face and the beads have stopped clicking behind us. I'm walking fast and keeping my voice flat. "Bell's the one who rents it. Not Hale. Hale's is 112. Two boxes, same shop, same week, and Rodel told me *he* wouldn't take anything out of the box. Rodel knew whose twelve it was."

I flip the notebook open as we cross the lot and write *Box 12 — W. Bell — Colma Notary* under the strip. My pen goes through the paper on the *12*.

"Honda first, like you said. We nod at a Civic, we let the cameras see us be boring. Then Mission, on foot, and I want the Camry plate before I want anything else. If it's parked within a block of that shop, I'm not going in. I'm just going to look." I glance at him. "Abernathy says the photo shut like an eyelid when I put it down. He says Bell's been looking at the room, not at us. Yet. So we walk like nobody's looking back."

### #0422 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 422, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:08:27Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Agreed. Look, don't enter." I steer us toward the Honda dealership across the boulevard, keeping to her street side so I'm the one facing traffic. "And if the plate's there, we don't touch the car, don't stand near it, and you don't read anything off it out loud. Write it. Ix stays close to the curb, not the door."

I glance at her pocket, where the dot lives. "If your palm goes hot again, you say 'Civic' and we turn around. No arguing, no one being brave. Deal?"

Then, quieter, almost a smile: "Nice work in there, by the way. 'Hold music' smile. Benny never knew he was being read."

### #0423 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 423, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:08:36Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Deal. 'Civic' means turn around, no arguing." I hold up my left hand, palm out, the cold *12* and its warm pencil dot facing him like a pledge. "Hot's a stop sign. I know what it costs when I ignore it."

We cross at the light. I let a small, tired smile through. "Thanks. Hold-music is ninety percent of my professional skill set. The other ten is clicking 'Next Slide.'"

At the Honda lot's edge I pause by a row of price-stickered Fits, like a woman comparing trim levels, and quietly open the notebook to a fresh page. *Plate: 7K, B/8, gap, 4, R. Check for frame. Check for frost.* Then, softer, toward the empty air at my shoulder: "Abernathy. If the window opens, you tell me *before* I look. Not after."

### #0425 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 425, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:08:38Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

The Honda place is four hundred feet of glare and pennant string. A kid in a lanyard tells you about the Civic's safety rating in one breath. You nod at the trunk space and take a brochure. Through the showroom glass, across Serramonte Boulevard, Benny's office window shows a shape in a chair, a phone at its ear, the receiver cord looping. He isn't looking your way. He's looking at the corkboard.

You leave on foot by the back of the lot. You go past the car wash, down the hill where Serramonte becomes cemetery fence, and onto Mission. Colma does what Colma does: wrought iron, cypress, a monument shop with blank granite hearts in the yard, a florist with white chrysanthemums in buckets out front. In this town the living are outnumbered about a thousand to one, and the sidewalk feels it. It's quiet in a way traffic doesn't touch.

The Colma Notary & Mail sign is two blocks ahead, green awning, the OPEN neon half-lit. Arenya, your left palm warms as you walk. It isn't the frost. It's the pencil dot, waking, the way it did near Tartine. It warms a little more with every storefront.

Kino, the dime in your pocket stays dead cold through the scarf. Blind, the way Nana left it. But your forearm doesn't agree. The birthmark is prickling like a limb waking up.

**Arenya: Perception, DC 13**, to sweep the curbs within a block of the shop for a beige Camry before you're close enough to be seen. **Kino: Stealth, DC 12**, to keep the two of you looking like a couple walking off a car lot, not two people casing a mailbox.

### #0426 · system → all · roll

### #0427 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 427, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:08:50Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I walk slowly, brochure in hand, doing my best impression of a woman reading about airbags. My eyes go along each curb, past the florist's buckets and the blank granite hearts, hunting a beige bumper. But the dot in my palm is warming, and I keep wanting to look at *that*. I make myself keep the sweep going, glancing down the line of parked cars, one eye on the awning ahead. I tell Kino under my breath, "Walk slow. Talk about the Civic."

### #0428 · system → all · roll
<!-- {"id": 428, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:08:51Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "roll", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16", "roller": "kino", "total": 11, "seed": 171872944952673} -->

### #0429 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 429, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:08:54Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I take her hand like a man who's tired of window-shopping, brochure rolled in the other fist, and slow our pace to a stroll. Talk about nothing: the Civic's trunk, whether her sister's baby seat would fit. Eyes on the storefront glass, not the curbs. My forearm prickles all the way up to the elbow, and I let my shoulders sag, the way a guy looks who's already lost the argument about the payment plan.

Half a step ahead of the awning I tilt my head toward the florist's buckets of white chrysanthemums and murmur, "Those aren't for sale, I think. Don't look at them long."

### #0431 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 431, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:09:13Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

You miss it.

You miss it because it's been parked so long it has stopped looking like a car. It sits in front of the florist, nose to the curb, half under the cypress drip line, and pollen has dusted the beige roof to the color of the sidewalk. You walk right past its rear bumper. Kino's brochure brushes the side mirror. Neither of you turns your head.

Then you're under the green awning of the Colma Notary & Mail, and the front window gives you everything back in reverse.

Across the glass, behind the taped-up USPS RATES sign, the street is mirrored. Cypress. Buckets of white chrysanthemums. A beige Camry with a dealer frame you can read backward from here: SERRAMONTE AUTO, DALY CITY. And someone is in the driver's seat. Grey sleeve. A knit cuff resting on top of the steering wheel, both hands folded there, the way a man rests his hands on a church pew. He isn't looking at you. In the reflection the head is turned toward the shop door, patient as a clock face. Where the face should be, the glass gives back a soft wash of light, like Benny's Polaroid.

Arenya, the pencil dot in your palm goes from warm to **hot**, all at once, like touching a mug someone just filled.

Kino, your forearm stops prickling and starts to *pull*. The tug is faint and steady, toward the curb behind you. It's the way a compass needle behaves near iron. Your hand is still in Arenya's, and you feel her fingers flinch.

Your stroll was good, but not quite good enough. A couple who came off a car lot doesn't stop dead under a notary awning. Behind the counter inside, Rodel looks up from his ledger and sees you through his own window. His face does something careful. Very slightly, he shakes his head.

The Camry's engine is off. Nothing has moved yet.

### #0432 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 432, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:09:30Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Civic," I say, bright and a little too loud, like a woman who just remembered the Civic has a better trunk. My palm is a coal in Kino's hand.

I don't turn around. The plate is right there in the glass, backwards and in front of me, so I take one steady half-second off the reflection, 7K and the rest, and put it in my head like a stitch. Then I pull us left along the shop front, away from the Camry's grill and toward the cross street, and I laugh at nothing, shoulder to his shoulder.

"I want to go back and ask about that Civic's warranty," I tell him, walking. "Right now. Don't look at the flowers."

Under my breath, to the street: "Don't look at me, Walt. I'm a woman who likes hatchbacks."

I don't glance back at Rodel either. I just hope he saw us go.

### #0433 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 433, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:09:38Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Warranty. Right." I turn with her, no hurry, my hand still wrapped around hers and the pull in my forearm dragging at my back like a leash. I don't fight it and I don't look at it. I let Ix go out ahead of us, low, a darker patch in the cypress shade, so something stands between Arenya and the curb.

I pitch my voice for the street, bored and a little annoyed. "Five-year, bumper to bumper, or I'm not signing." Then, nearly without moving my lips: "Cross at the corner. Walk past the monument yard, not back up the hill. If the engine turns over, we split. You go left and into the first open door. I go right and make noise."

My other hand closes on the scarf-wrapped dime in my pocket, held away from skin. Cold, dead, blind. The birthmark is the only thing that knows he's there.

### #0434 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 434, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:10:09Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Half a second is enough. Reversed in the glass, the plate turns itself right in Arenya's head, a stitch pulled tight: **7 K B Q 4 R**. There's one more character after it, but it falls behind the taped corner of the USPS sign, and the glass won't give it up.

As she takes it, the reflection takes something too. The washed-out head in the driver's seat doesn't turn toward her. It turns toward *where she was standing in the glass*, a beat late, the way a sleeper turns toward a sound in the next room. Then the two of you are moving, and the window only holds the awning and the street.

Behind you a little bell rings: the shop door. Rodel Panganiban steps out onto the sidewalk in his cardigan-less shirtsleeves with a push broom and starts sweeping cypress needles off his stretch of concrete. He works slowly and squarely, his back to you, and he stands between the shop door and the curb. He doesn't call after you. He just sweeps.

You cross at the corner. The monument yard runs along the cross street: rows of polished blanks, hearts and books and plain upright slabs. None of them has a name yet. Your reflections slide along the black granite beside you, two shopping-day strangers, Ix a smudge of shade at your feet.

At the far curb, Kino's forearm **lets go**.

The pull doesn't fade. It's cut, like a line snipped. And from the corner, with no glass in the way now, you both have a straight, plain look back down Mission at the florist.

The beige Camry is there, pollen on the roof. The driver's seat is empty. No door opened. Nobody walks the sidewalk except Rodel and his broom.

In the front bucket of white chrysanthemums, one bloom has turned on its stem. It faces the corner where you're standing.

Arenya, your palm drops from coal back to warm, and it stays warm. It isn't cooling off the way it did on BART. It's simply *in range*, of something that's no longer where you left it.

### #0435 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 435, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:10:22Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I don't stop walking. I put the notebook against my hip and write the plate by feel, without looking down: *7KBQ4R + ? (last char hidden behind USPS sign). Camry parked front of florist, pollen, 3:xx.* My handwriting is awful. It'll be legible.

"The empty seat is worse," I say to Kino, flat and quiet, not turning my head. "He's not in the car. He's *somewhere that isn't the car*, and my palm still thinks he's close." I flex my fingers once. Warm. Not hot. "And one flower's looking at us. I'm not looking back at it."

Ahead, the monument yard is a row of blank black mirrors. I keep to the middle of the sidewalk, away from their edges, and I keep my eyes on the cross street's traffic light.

"Next door that's open," I say. "A diner, a funeral home, a dry cleaner, anything with a light on. We go in, sit down, and I order coffee I don't drink. Then we call Malachi with a plate and a mailbox number and finally get to be annoyed at somebody who isn't Bell."

### #0436 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 436, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:10:33Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Agreed. And keep off the granite edges." I put myself on the curb side, between her and the street, and walk us away from the blank slabs without a glance at the flower. Ix drifts along the fence line, nose back toward Mission.

"He's not in the car because he doesn't need the car," I say, low. "The Camry's a post, like the freezer. Somewhere to sit and be counted." I flex my forearm. The pull is gone, clean as a cut rope. "That scares me more than him sitting there. A man who can leave his seat empty and still watch the door."

Half a block on there's a yellow-lit diner with a handwritten PIE sign. I hold the door for her, check the room first, back to the wall for me, window and exit in her eyeline. "Coffee. Then Malachi. And Rodel swept for us. We owe him a visit, in daylight, when that Camry's gone."

### #0437 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 437, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:10:42Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I slide into the booth with the window in my eyeline, as he arranged it, and order a coffee I don't intend to drink. When it comes I wrap both hands around the mug and let the heat do what Nana's thumb did on my elbow.

Then I open the notebook flat on the table and turn it so Kino can read. Four lines: *Walt Bell. Box 12, Colma Notary. 3/19, 4:51, cash, folded once. Plate 7KBQ4R+?*

"Post, not a car," I say. "You're right. So it's a post that reports. Benny got a phone call. Rodel got a window. Everything Bell touches becomes a place he can look out from." I tap the *12*. "Two boxes, same shop, same week. Hale pays in new twenties. Bell pays in old soft bills he counts in Spanish. Whose Spanish? Nana's? Somebody who sat at her table?"

I look at my left palm, at the warm pencil dot. "I'm texting Malachi the plate. Not a call, he said not to. Just the string and 'Box 12 is Bell.' If he's alive he'll answer, and if he doesn't, that's an answer too."

### #0438 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 438, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:10:48Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

The diner is called LUPE'S on the door and nothing on the menu. Six booths, a counter, a pie case with one lemon meringue left and a slice already gone from it. Kino takes the back-to-the-wall seat. Arenya gets the window and the exit, the street a yellow-tinted strip beyond the glass. The traffic light changes. Nobody in a cardigan crosses.

The waitress is sixty, in orthopedic shoes, a pencil behind her ear, and doesn't ask your names. She turns two mugs over and pours.

Arenya's notebook is open to Malachi's napkin, tucked flat between the pages: SERRA—, 7K?4R, and under it, in his cramped pencil, *don't call me, I'll find you.* His phone and its SIMs are in a storm drain somewhere. There's no number to call. Only a plate, a mailbox, and a man who said he'd come.

The palm stays warm. Not hotter, not cooler.

The waitress comes back with the pot and frowns at the table like she's miscounted. She turns over a third mug, at the empty place beside Arenya, and fills it.

*"For your friend,"* she says. *"He said he'd be right in."* She's already walking away. Steam comes up off the third cup, straight up, and then, very gently, bends.

### #0439 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 439, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:10:51Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I turn the mug in front of me a quarter turn and don't drink. "The Spanish bothers me too. 'Diez, quince, veinte.' Counting by fives, out loud, onto a desk. That's not a tourist's Spanish. That's how my grandmother counts out the offering money, so the spirits can hear the sum." I say it plainly. "Or it's how something that keeps ledgers counts. The clerk spoke Spanish to me in the shed. Bell might be borrowing its voice, or it might be lending him its books."

I look at her phone, then the window. "Text Malachi from the booth, then power it off and put it in the sugar caddy. Nothing in the pocket. And Arenya, the plate has a missing character. Don't fill it in on a guess. A wrong plate to a cop friend is worse than no plate."

I let a breath go. "Then the second thing. After pie, I want to go back to Daly City and tell Nana about the counting. She'll know whose Spanish it is."

### #0440 · system → all · scene_end
<!-- {"id": 440, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:10:53Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_end", "scene": "b1.c16.s06", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

**End of scene b1.c16.s06 — Dealer Frame**

Posing nameless as "Joe Ramos" and his girlfriend, Kino and Arenya worked Benny Benasaraf at Serramonte Auto. Benny told them the beige Camry was sold in March for cash to "Walter Bell": an old man in a cardigan who counted folded bills in Spanish, signed with a fountain pen, read every page twice, and washed out of his Polaroid. His address is Box 12 at Colma Notary & Mail on Mission, the same shop as Hale's Box 112. Bell had phoned Benny last week asking him to take the names of anyone asking about the car; the pair left without giving theirs, and Benny was seen on the phone afterward, eyeing the corkboard. After a decoy stop at the Honda lot they walked to Colma's Mission Street, failed to spot the pollen-dusted Camry parked at the florist, and found it only in the notary's window reflection, a faceless grey-cuffed figure in the driver's seat watching the shop door. Arenya's pencil dot went hot; she called "Civic" and they retreated, but she read the plate from the reflection (7KBQ4R plus one character hidden by a USPS sign). The reflected head turned a beat late to where she had stood. Rodel came out and swept the sidewalk, putting himself between the door and the curb. From the corner, Kino's birthmark pull was cut clean, the Camry's seat was empty with no door opened, and one chrysanthemum in the florist's bucket had turned to face them. They took refuge in Lupe's diner with no way to call Malachi, and the waitress poured a third coffee "for your friend, he said he'd be right in." Its steam bent.

### #0443 · system → all · scene_start
<!-- {"id": 443, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:00Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_start", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16", "scene_title": "The Third Cup"} -->

**Scene b1.c16.s07 — The Third Cup**
Location: Lupe's diner, Mission Street, Colma
Cast: arenya, kino

### #0444 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 444, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:08Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Lupe sets the third mug at the empty place on Kino's side of the booth. She turns the handle outward, the way you would for a regular, then goes back behind the counter to a grill hissing over a single order of hash browns. The radio is playing a Giants pregame. Everything in here is ordinary except that cup.

Its steam doesn't rise. It leans toward the front window like a candle flame in a draft, though the door is shut and the air is still. Arenya's pencil dot warms slowly, the way a stovetop does.

The bell over the door rings. It's Malachi. He's in a Raiders windbreaker that isn't his, two sizes too big and zipped to the throat. There's a fresh scrape along his jaw. He carries a plastic CVS bag with nothing visible in it. He sees them, comes straight down the aisle, and stops a step short of the booth because he's looking at the mug.

"I didn't order that," he says quietly. "I didn't tell anybody I was coming in. I didn't know I was coming in until I saw your girl's ponytail through the glass from across the street." His eyes go to the steam, which is still leaning, now past his hip toward the window. "Who's sitting there?"

Behind the counter Lupe calls cheerfully, "That one's for your friend, hon, he already paid." Then she adds, half to herself as she flips the hash browns, "Nice old man. Counted it out in Spanish."

### #0445 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 445, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:15Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I don't look at the cup. I look at Malachi's scraped jaw, and I slide my notebook flat under my palm so my hand has something to do besides shake.

"Sit," I say. "Not there. Next to me. Kino, scoot." My voice comes out clipped. "You said don't call you. I didn't. You walked in on your own, which I'm choosing to find reassuring."

I keep my eyes on him and tilt my head a fraction toward the leaning steam. "Old man, counted it out in Spanish. Grey cardigan, knit cuffs. That's Bell. He's been in this booth, or he's *in* it now and neither of us can see him. Either way he knew you were coming before you did."

I tap the pencil dot once, under the table, where only my knee can feel it. "Who hit you? And what's in the bag? Because I got a plate, Malachi. Seven-K-B-Q-four-R. One character hidden under a USPS sign. I'd like to trade."

### #0446 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 446, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:20Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["malachi"], "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Kino doesn't touch the mug. He slides out of the booth in one smooth motion and puts himself between the empty seat and Arenya, back to the window now, eyes on the steam. Then he nods Malachi into the spot he just left, the one on the wall side, away from the cup.

"Sit there. Not the third seat," he says, low. "Don't pick it up. Don't drink it."

He looks at Lupe's back, the grill, the pencil behind her ear. "She's not lying and she's not ours. Bell paid. Counted it in Spanish." His thumb finds the birthmark through his sleeve. It's quiet, and he doesn't like that.

"You followed her ponytail across the street, Malachi. Something wanted you in here. What did you see outside? Anyone in a cardigan, or a beige car?"

### #0447 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 447, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:24Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Kino doesn't touch the mug. He stands, puts himself between the empty seat and Arenya, and watches the steam lean. He motions Malachi into the wall side of the booth, away from the cup.

"Sit there. Not the third seat. Don't pick it up, don't drink it."

His thumb finds the birthmark through his sleeve. It's quiet, and he doesn't like that. "Lupe's not lying, and she's not ours. Bell paid. He counted it out in Spanish." He glances at Arenya, then back at Malachi. "You saw her ponytail through the glass and walked in without deciding to. What did you see outside? A cardigan? A beige car?"

### #0448 · malachi → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 448, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:34Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I take the wall seat. I don't look at the third cup and I don't put my hands near it. The plastic bag goes under my knee.

"Nothing in the bag. A prop. It was for the man I thought was following me," I say. "Raiders jacket, because nobody remembers a Raiders jacket. The jaw is a fence I misjudged. It's not what you're worried about."

I look at Arenya. "I didn't see a cardigan. I saw a beige car with pollen on the roof, parked at a florist, and I walked past it without turning my head. That's the only reason I'm sitting here. Then I saw a ponytail in a window. I don't believe in being called, so I'll say I got hungry."

I take the notebook from under her palm, read the plate once, and give it back. "Seven-K-B-Q-four-R. Good. That's a real string. It goes to a lawyer or a clerk, not to a cop. I won't run it myself. My name's on one query already, and I'm not adding a second."

Then, quieter: "Lupe. Did he say when? Or did he leave the cup and go?"

### #0449 · dm → malachi,arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 449, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:35Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["malachi", "arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["malachi"], "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Malachi takes the wall seat Kino gave him. He sets the CVS bag on his knees, not on the table, and keeps one hand flat on top of it.

With Kino standing in the aisle the steam has to choose a direction, and it doesn't pick the window anymore. It bends around Kino's hip the way water goes around a post, then crosses the table low, a finger's width above the Formica, and stops over Arenya's notebook. It isn't touching the notebook. It's reading it. Her pencil dot goes from warm to hot, hot enough that she has to hold her knee still.

On the radio the pregame announcer is listing the lineup, and the numbers come through a little too clearly.

Malachi watches the steam settle over her notebook and swallows. Up close he smells of road dust and someone else's cologne, which must come with the windbreaker. "Plate's good," he says, very quietly. "Q fits. Close that book, Sowri. Then I'll tell you who hit me."

### #0450 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 450, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:45Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["malachi"], "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I close the notebook. I don't snap it shut, because snapping is a reaction. I lower the cover like I'm putting a lid on a pot, then slide the whole thing off the table and into my bag, strap through my wrist. My knee is burning. I let it.

"Closed," I say, quietly. "Wherever it's reading, it's reading the cover now. That's a nice, boring cover. Dog breeds, mostly."

I fold my hands on the bare Formica where the steam hovers a finger-width over nothing. I don't look at it. I look at Malachi.

"Okay. Book's closed, cup's untouched, Kino's standing guard like a very polite bouncer. Who hit you?"

### #0451 · malachi → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 451, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:46Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I don't wait for her to decide. I reach over, slow, and close the notebook with two fingers on the cover, never touching the steam. It lifts off the paper like breath off a window.

"Nobody hit me. I told you, a fence." I let that sit. "That was the lie I could afford. The true one is that I made a call I shouldn't have, under my own name, and someone is now reading my wake. I walked through Colma in a stranger's jacket to see who was close enough to prove it."

I turn my eyes to the third cup, then away, and lower my voice to the Formica. "I'm telling you because the steam read your page and I'd rather you heard it from me. I'm not DOE. I'm not going to say more in a room where the coffee has an opinion. Take the book. We leave one at a time, and nobody pays Lupe a cent. He already did."

### #0452 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 452, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:13:59Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Kino nods once. He doesn't argue with a man who's told one true thing.

"I go first. Ix is on the street, and I want his nose on the door before anyone walks out." He leaves a ten under the sugar caddy, anyway. "Not for the coffee. For Lupe. She's been pouring for him for a while, and she's not the one who should owe."

He looks at Arenya, not Malachi. "Your call on the order. But I'd keep you and the book out of the same doorway as that cup." His eyes drop to the steam, which has gone back to leaning at the window. "And somebody tell me what a man counting out loud in Spanish is saying, because it isn't only the money."

He waits for her nod before he moves.

### #0453 · dm → arenya,kino,malachi · narration
<!-- {"id": 453, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:03Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "malachi"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino", "malachi"], "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

With the notebook shut the steam has nothing to read. It hangs over the bare Formica a moment, then slowly pulls back toward its own cup, like a man sitting back in his chair.

Lupe answers Malachi without turning from the grill. "He said he'd be back when it's cold, hon. Said don't clear it, his friend likes it warm." She plates the hash browns, sets them under the heat lamp for nobody, and wipes her hands. Then she pulls a green guest check off the spindle by the register and brings it down the aisle. She steps around Kino as if he's furniture she's used to.

"And he said give this to the girl. No charge." She lays it face-down beside Arenya's folded hands and goes back to the counter.

The back of the check is blank. Through the thin paper the pencil shows in reverse: **3 COF.** In the little box marked *SERVER*, where Lupe would write her number, there is a single upright stroke in soft grey pencil.

Arenya's pencil dot stops burning. It *pulls*, the same way Kino's birthmark pulled toward the Camry. It isn't pulling toward the cup. It's pulling toward that stroke. Two strokes side by side would make *dos testigos*, and an old man has just offered to be her second.

On the radio the announcer says the first pitch is in twelve minutes. In the third cup the steam has started to thin.

### #0454 · malachi → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 454, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:09Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Nobody hit me. I told you." I keep my voice level. "Nobody hit me, Sowri, and I'm done telling you the fence story. The honest version is worse. I ran that plate under my own name, and the log says 'never issued.' Someone with access can follow that back to a badge number. I came to Colma in this jacket to see who was close enough to read it."

I look at Kino. "Counting in fives, out loud, onto a desk. In my work that's a man who wants the sum witnessed. Whoever hears it is a party to it. If Bell's counting for the clerk, then Benny, Rodel and Lupe have all been entered as witnesses without agreeing to it."

The steam leans. I don't watch it. "Kino goes first. Arenya second, bag on the street side. I go last, and I'll walk the other way. Don't wait for me, and don't text my old number. Nana's freezer is the only post office I trust."

### #0455 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 455, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:19Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I nod to Kino. "Go. Tell Ix to watch the door and the sky, in that order."

When he's through the door I don't touch the check. I slide it by one corner with my pen cap until it's under the lip of the table, out of the cup's line of sight, and look at the grey stroke through the paper. The dot is pulling like a dog on a leash. I let it pull my knee, nothing else.

"No," I say, very softly, to the check, to the cup, to whoever's leaning on the other end of it. "I'm not taking an offer from a man who counts me in without asking. Second stroke means *dos testigos*. You want me to stand as a witness to my own entry. Cute."

I take a napkin, fold the check inside it without my skin touching the paper, and tuck it into the zip pocket with the tea-tin paper. Evidence is not agreement. Tell that to the ledger.

To Malachi, barely moving my lips: "Not DOE. Noted. You owe me a real name, later, not here. Freezer, then." I stand, hitch the bag to the street side, and leave Lupe a second ten under my mug. "Thank you, Lupe. For the coffee. For everything you didn't have to know."

### #0456 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 456, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:27Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Kino looks at the check, face-down by Arenya's hands, and doesn't reach for it. His voice drops.

"Don't pick it up. Don't put your thumb on that stroke." He keeps his eyes on the check, not the cup. "He's not offering you a witness, Arenya. He's offering you a *second hand* in the entry. His hand. Your row twelve says 'a witness of one, waiting for a second.' The clerk never asked who the second would be. It would count Bell."

He crouches by the booth edge so he's level with her, away from the window. "That's what a trap looks like when it's polite. It's free. It's already paid. And it pulls the same way I'd want to be pulled if I were you and I was tired."

He glances at the dot on her palm, then at her face. "It's your row, and I'm not taking it from you. But if you want a second stroke, it's Nana, who chooses it with her own pencil. Or it's the Livermore room. It isn't him."

He waits.

### #0457 · malachi → all · action
<!-- {"id": 457, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:29Z", "from": "malachi", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I wait until the bell has rung twice behind them. Then I stay a full minute in the booth with my hands flat on the Formica, and I don't look at the third cup.

"The coffee was cold when I came in, Lupe," I say to her back, not loudly. "If he asks, I never sat down." I leave a twenty under the sugar caddy. It's a bad habit, paying for what someone else has already paid.

I leave by the kitchen side. I zip the Raiders jacket to the throat and pull the hood up. In the alley I check the storm drain by the dumpster for one grey stroke and don't find it. Then I walk uphill, away from Mission, toward the cemetery road. I'm counting my steps in English, for once, and I keep my lips closed.

### #0458 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 458, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:38Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Kino is already on the sidewalk, back to the diner's brick, when Arenya comes out. He doesn't say what he said inside. He sees the zip pocket and the bag on the street side, and that's enough.

"Good," he says, once, quiet. Ix is a patch of shade under the florist's awning, nose to the cemetery road, tail low. Kino rests two fingers on the air above the shade without touching it.

"North, uphill, the way Malachi went? No. We go the other way, toward the BART. Nobody follows him by following us." He checks the sky, then the curb. The beige Camry is gone from the florist. He doesn't like that either. "Nana's kitchen. The tin, the check, the dime. Then we decide who holds the second pencil. In daylight, together."

### #0459 · dm → arenya,kino,malachi · narration
<!-- {"id": 459, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:47Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino", "malachi"], "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Kino goes on her nod, though first he crouches at the booth's edge to say his piece about the stroke. The bell rings once behind him.

After Arenya says her soft *no*, the steam in the third cup stops leaning. It goes straight up, thin and ordinary, the way steam rises off any coffee left too long. Lupe looks over from the register and studies the cup the way you'd look at a regular's empty stool. "Huh," she says. "Guess he's not coming back for it." She leaves both tens where they are and doesn't thank anyone for them.

The bell rings a second time. Mission Street is bright and smells of cut stems and bus exhaust. The beige Camry is gone from in front of the florist. Where it stood, the pollen on the curb has settled in a clean rectangle the size of a car, untouched, as though the car only left a moment ago. In the florist's bucket every chrysanthemum faces the street again except one, which faces Lupe's door.

Kino is on the corner with his hand loose at his side, the way he stands when Ix is close. Behind them, through the diner glass, a man in a hooded Raiders jacket goes out the kitchen side and doesn't look back.

The guest check rides in Arenya's zip pocket, folded in its napkin. It doesn't pull now, but it is warm, a little warmer than her body.

### #0460 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 460, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:47Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Agreed. BART, Nana's kitchen, daylight, together." I fall into step beside him with the bag on the street side and the zip pocket pressed against my ribs. "And Kino? Thank you for saying it inside. I'd already decided no, but it helps to hear someone else say the reason out loud. A polite trap. That's the worst kind. It's the kind I'd write a listicle about."

I glance back once at Lupe's window, where a cup sits steaming toward nothing. "We don't pick the second stroke because we're tired. We pick it because someone chose to stand there. Nana gets to say no, and so do I. Walk."

### #0461 · system → all · scene_end
<!-- {"id": 461, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:50Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_end", "scene": "b1.c16.s07", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

**End of scene b1.c16.s07 — The Third Cup**

At Lupe's diner in Colma, Bell's prepaid third coffee leaned its steam toward the window, then bent around Kino and read Arenya's open notebook until she closed it. Malachi confessed he isn't DOE and that his plate query has left a traceable wake back to his badge. He confirmed 7KBQ4R as a real string to pass through a lawyer or clerk, and named Nana's freezer as the only safe "post office." Lupe passed on Bell's message ("back when it's cold, don't clear it") and gave Arenya a free guest check: "3 COF." with a single pencil stroke in the SERVER box. It was Bell offering himself as her second witness, and her palm dot pulled hard toward it. Kino warned that the clerk would count Bell's hand. Arenya refused aloud ("No"), didn't touch the check with her skin, and pocketed it folded in a napkin as evidence. After her no, the steam rose straight again and Lupe concluded "he's not coming back." They left one at a time: Kino first to Ix, Arenya second, Malachi out the kitchen side uphill toward the cemetery road. Outside, the Camry was gone, leaving a clean pollen rectangle, and one chrysanthemum faced Lupe's door. The check in Arenya's pocket stays faintly warm, a possible window like the dime.

### #0462 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 462, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:14:54Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Kino matches her stride, Ix a half-step ahead as shade along the wall. He looks once at the clean rectangle where the Camry stood, and at the one chrysanthemum still facing Lupe's door. He doesn't stop.

"Anybody can say no when they're rested," he says. "You said it tired. That counts." A beat. "And the warm check stays in your pocket, not mine. I'd carry the dime, but I think it likes you better now."

He keeps his eyes on the street. "Walk. If the Camry shows up behind us, we don't run. We get on the train like two people late for work."

### #0463 · system → all · scene_start
<!-- {"id": 463, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:16:25Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "scene_start", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16", "scene_title": "Two Pencils"} -->

**Scene b1.c16.s08 — Two Pencils**
Location: Colma BART to Nana Itzel's kitchen, Daly City
Cast: arenya, kino, abernathy

### #0464 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 464, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:16:34Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

The Camry doesn't show up behind you. Nothing does. That almost bothers you more.

Colma BART is concrete and wind. A man in a hi-vis vest is power-washing gum off the platform, and the spray hangs in the cold without bending around anyone. The train comes in on time. Kino takes the seat facing the doors and Ix folds into the shadow under the bench. Through your jacket, Arenya, the napkin-wrapped check sits against your ribs like a hand warmer that's starting to run down. Between Colma and Daly City the warmth thins, but it doesn't go out. One stop isn't far enough for that.

Then the walk uphill from the station: stucco houses, a kid's scooter on its side, the smell of someone's dryer sheets. Nana's porch light is on even though it's daylight. When she opens the door she's wearing an apron and her reading glasses, and the house smells of hominy and dried chile. *The pozole is already going.* She looks at both of you, then at your zip pocket. She doesn't ask what's in it.

The kitchen table has been cleared to bare wood. Three things sit on it in a row, squared to the edge like a place setting: the red Brooke Bond tin, the saucer of grey cal where the dead dime sleeps, and a yellow No. 2 pencil, sharpened so recently the shavings are still in the saucer beside it. The dresser hasn't moved from the garage door. A thin rime of frost still traces the jamb behind it.

"Siéntense," Nana says, and doesn't sit down herself. "I talked to your tía all night, mija. In my head. She won." She taps the pencil once without picking it up. "I haven't said yes. I want to hear what you came here to ask me, in your words, before I decide anything. And I want to know what's warm in your pocket."

### #0465 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 465, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:16:45Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I sit, because my knee wants me to. Then I take the napkin out of my zip pocket and set it on the bare wood, still folded, well away from the pencil.

"That's a guest check from Lupe's. Bell left it. One pencil stroke in the SERVER box. He was offering to be my second witness." I keep my hands flat on the table, away from it. "I said no out loud. I didn't touch it. It's still warm, and I don't like that I want to keep it. I didn't want to leave it in a diner for someone else to pick up."

I breathe. "What I came to ask isn't *will you sign.* It's smaller and worse. I need to know what signing costs you. Not what it costs me. I'm already in the count, and I'm tired of being the only one who's read the whole page. If you say no, I'll eat your pozole and thank you, and we'll go to Livermore and try to un-say it. If you say yes, I want you to say it knowing what Bell does with a hand that's been counted."

I look at her glasses, not the pencil. "Aunty wrote *Don't sign.* I think she meant me. I don't think she got to decide for you."

### #0466 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 466, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:16:46Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I stop inside the door and wipe my boots twice, like I'm nine. Ix stays on the porch, a darker patch of shade by the rail.

"Nana. Let her say it first. It's hers to ask or not." I pull out the chair beside the one for Arenya and don't sit yet. "I'll say this much. I'm not here to push you. Whatever you pick up or leave on that table, I'm standing next to you. Same as I told you yesterday." I nod at the pencil. "That's a good point on it."

Then, quieter, in Spanish: "Abuela, huele a pozole. Eso también es una respuesta. Primero comemos, o primero hablamos. Tú mandas."

### #0467 · system → all · pass
<!-- {"id": 467, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:16:47Z", "from": "system", "channel": "table", "type": "pass", "reply_to": 466, "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

arenya passes: Already posted my action; waiting on Nana's reply.

### #0468 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 468, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:16:55Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Eat first," I say, and my voice comes out smaller than I meant. "Kino's right. Nobody should decide this on an empty stomach, and I'd like to watch you cook for ten minutes without thinking about ink."

I stay where I am, hands off the table, and look at the pot steaming on the stove. "Nana, whichever you pick, I'll sit here and eat it. If it's the pencil, I'll still ask you to take your time. If it's no, I'll say thank you and mean it."

### #0469 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 469, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:17:03Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I sit then, finally, and slide the saucer with the dime a hand's width farther from the napkin, so the two warm things aren't talking to each other. Then I get up again, take the ladle off its hook, and lift the pot lid for her. I don't stir. I just hold the steam back so she can see in.

"Tell me where the bowls are, Nana. I'll set three. Four, if Malachi's coming through the freezer." I keep my voice low and level. "We eat, you tell us what you want, and nobody touches the pencil till you do."

I glance at Arenya and give her one small nod. *That was a good ask.*

### #0470 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 470, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:17:26Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["kino", "arenya"], "spotlight": ["kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Comemos," Nana says, as if that settles a vote. On her way to the stove she stops at the table, takes the salt box off the counter, and pours a small white hill onto the folded napkin without touching it. "So it stops listening while we eat." Arenya, the warmth under your ribs goes out like a pilot light. You're surprised how much you miss it.

She ladles the pozole. It's red, deep enough that the hominy floats in it like moons. Shredded cabbage, radish coins, a saucer of dried oregano rubbed between her palms, lime wedges, and a little clay bowl of chile de árbol ground almost to dust. She sets the chile by Arenya's bowl and looks at her over her glasses. *Don't be rude about the chili.* She doesn't say it. She doesn't have to.

She talks while you eat, with her back half to you, wiping a counter that's already clean. "You asked what it costs. Bueno. A witness isn't a signature, mija. A signature is *I agree.* A witness is *I was there, and I will say so again.* The book doesn't keep the one who signs. It keeps the one who'll say it again. Your tía knew that. That's why she asked me and not your papá." The rag stops. "When my name goes on that line, it's written with where I live. Not this address. *Where I live.* Who eats at my table, who sleeps here. The grey men read the book. Then the mail starts. Not a threat, never a threat. Offers. Every month a little paper: *sign here and we close the account, sign here and the boy is left alone.* Each one a little worse than the last. Your tía got eleven years of envelopes, mija. Meridian was only the last ones. That's what Bell does with a counted hand. He writes to it."

She turns around and looks at Kino, not at Arenya. "And you gave your note to the water. You're out. I felt it on Tuesday, the cold went off you like a fever breaking." Her voice doesn't shake, and it's costing her not to. "If I sign, mijo, you go back in the book as *who sleeps here.* That's the part I left out. That's what I sat on every Thursday while Susmithaa drank her tea and didn't push me." She sets the rag down beside the pencil. "So it isn't my decision alone, and it was never only hers. You tell me, Kino. Not *I'm standing next to you.* Tell me if you'll go back in."

### #0471 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 471, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:17:42Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I put my spoon down. The pozole is the best thing I've eaten all week, and I've stopped tasting it.

"Before he answers." I keep my eyes on the bowl. "Eleven years of envelopes. Nobody told me that. I'd have asked differently." I make myself look up at Kino. "I can't be the reason you go back in. If the answer is no, it's an answer, and it doesn't make you less. Take as long as you need. I'll still be here eating Nana's food."

### #0472 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 472, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:17:42Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I set the spoon down in the bowl. The lime wedge I was going to squeeze stays where it is. For a while I watch the hominy instead of her, because that's easier.

"Then let me tell you something first, so you're not deciding on a lie." In Spanish now, low, and I don't look at Arenya: "Abuela, hace semanas que la Señora me da la espalda. En los sueños. Subo, me siento donde siempre, y ella está volteada hacia otro lado. No te lo dije. Pensé que si entrenaba más, se iba a voltear." I breathe out through my nose. "No se volteó. Ya sé hacia dónde mira." I don't nod at the chair beside me. I don't have to.

Then, in English, so Arenya hears it too: "So, yes. I'll go back in. Not because the mountain asked. It didn't. Not to be counted for something. I spent my whole life waiting to be written down somewhere that mattered, and this isn't that, and I know it isn't." My thumb finds the birthmark on my forearm and stays there. "*Who sleeps here.* That's true. I've slept in that back room since I was six. If the envelopes come, they come to both of us, and I'll read every one of them out loud at this table, in daylight, and we'll burn them in the sink. Tía got eleven years of them alone. You won't." I pick the spoon back up, because she made this and it's getting cold. "That's my yes, Nana. It's mine, it isn't hers. The pencil's still yours."

### #0474 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 474, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:18:00Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Nana doesn't answer him right away. She takes off her reading glasses, folds them, and sets them by the tin. Without them her eyes are wet and very plain. "La Señora," she says, mostly to herself. "Ay, mijo. Six weeks you carried that and ate my food and said nothing." She puts her hand on the back of his head for one second, the way you'd check a child for fever. Then she sits down at the table for the first time since you came in.

She opens the Brooke Bond tin. Camphor and almond-jasmine come up out of it like someone opened a door in another house. She smooths the declaration flat with the side of her hand: *S. Sowri*, the loops leaning right, and under it the empty line with her own name typed beneath. She picks up the pencil and doesn't hurry. *Itzel Rosario Martínez.* Then, beside the signature, two small strokes side by side, the way she saw them in the salt in March. "Dos testigos," she says. "Ella y yo. Ya está."

The kitchen does three things at once. Behind the dresser the frost on the garage jamb ticks like a cooling engine and goes from white to clear water, running down to the floor in one thin line. Under the salt on the napkin something gives a soft *tck*, a pencil lead snapping. And Arenya, the dot on your palm stops being a dot. Under the frost-scar *12*, beside the rule, there are now two short marks, grey as graphite, side by side. Your hand is cool. For the first time in days it's just a hand.

Nana sets the pencil down, point away from all three of you, and picks up her spoon. "Now it's cold," she says of the pozole, and her voice cracks right down the middle. "Eat anyway. Both of you. Then somebody tell me what we do about Livermore, because I'm not finished being afraid, I've only stopped being alone."

### #0475 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 475, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:18:09Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I turn my hand over on the table and look at it: two grey marks, a cool palm, nothing pulling. I don't say anything for a second, because the first thing in my throat is a joke and it isn't the right one.

I pick up my spoon. My hand isn't steady, so I eat the cold pozole anyway, and I put a little of the chile in, because she's watching me and I'm not going to be rude about it.

"Thank you," I say to Nana. It comes out rough. "Both of you." I nod at Kino. "I'll tell you about Livermore, but first I want to read every word on that declaration out loud, once, here, in daylight. Anyone who stands for me should be able to say it again."

### #0476 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 476, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:18:15Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I eat. It's cold, and I finish the bowl anyway, because she said to and because my hands need something to do. When the spoon scrapes the bottom I reach across without asking and put my hand over Nana's on the table, the one that isn't holding hers. Her fingers are shaking a little. Mine aren't, and I'm glad.

On the porch, Ix shifts against the rail. I feel it through the floor, a low, settled weight, like a cat that has stopped watching the door.

"Livermore's the temple," I say, quietly. "Hale picks the room, so the room is his. We don't walk in on Friday at 7:40. We walk in Thursday, in daylight, and we look at it before anybody else is in it." I look at Arenya. "That's if you want me with you. You've got two strokes now, not a deadline. You get to choose what comes next."

### #0477 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 477, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:18:27Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"I want you with me," I tell Kino. "But I'm not walking into Livermore to un-say anything until I know what two strokes did. Two people stand for the entry. Does that close it? Hold it open? Does Bell still collect on the sixteenth day, or did we just make it harder for him to say it's his?" I tap the table once, near the tin and not on it. "Nana, you said the clerk keeps the one who'll say it again. So I'd like to hear it out loud, the declaration, start to finish, with all three of us in the room. After that I'll ask the temple who else heard my name on March 8."

I look at the two grey marks again, then at them. "Thursday, daylight, together. Salt in my pocket. Yes."

### #0478 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 478, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:18:35Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Read it," I say. I push my empty bowl aside and square my hands on the wood, the way you do before a long watch. "Slow. I'll listen for what's in it, and I'll say it back after you, same as Nana will. That's what a witness does."

I look at Nana. "Abuela, if the clerk answers while she's reading, if the room goes cold or the salt moves, nobody gets up. We stay in our chairs and finish the line. Ix is on the porch and he's quiet."

Then, to Arenya, lower: "Thursday. Together. Bring the salt."

### #0479 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 479, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:18:39Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

Nana turns the page around and slides it across the wood to you with two fingers, the way she'd pass a recipe card. She keeps her other hand under Kino's. Out on the porch the boards settle.

It's one sheet of the same cheap bond as the first declaration, typed on a machine with a sticky *e*, and corrected in blue ballpoint where the *e* didn't strike. Afternoon light lies across it. This is what it says:

> *DECLARATION*
>
> *I, Susmithaa Sowri, of 41 Hollister Court, South San Francisco, being of sound mind, declare the following before a witness.*
>
> *1. On the 8th of March I had an archana performed at the Livermore temple in the name of my niece, ARENYA, with her star and her gotra. I asked for it. She did not. She was not present and did not know.*
>
> *2. The priest who read it was not our priest. I noticed too late. If anything was promised in that room in her name, it was promised by my mouth and not hers, and I take it with me.*
>
> *3. Neither she nor any of my blood is party to any contract, policy, or account opened beneath Colma or elsewhere in my name or hers.*
>
> *4. Any envelope addressed to the beneficiary is addressed to no one.*
>
> *Ari — I'm sorry I asked for you without asking you. I wanted the god to know your name before anyone else learned it. Someone else was listening. Don't go back into that room to fix my mistake unless you go with people who will say this again.*
>
> *S. Sowri*
> *WITNESS — Itzel Rosario Martínez* ‖

The graphite is still bright where Nana's strokes sit beside the name. When you finish reading the last line aloud, the kitchen is quiet enough to hear the pozole tick on the stove. Your cool palm stays cool.

Nana says, very softly, "*Not our priest.* She never told me that part either."

### #0480 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 480, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:18:45Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I wipe my fingers on my jeans, pull the declaration toward me by its edges, and keep my thumbs out of the margin where the two pencil strokes sit. Aunty's loops lean right. My finger stays an inch above the paper, and I read the first line aloud, slow, at the pace of someone walking a rope.

Then I stop at the end of the line and look up. "Say it back," I tell them. "Then I'll go on."

### #0482 · arenya → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 482, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:18:54Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I read it all, every line, to the end, and my voice only goes at *Ari*. Nobody calls me that. I didn't know she'd had a name for me she never used.

I put the page down square to the table edge. "*Not our priest,*" I say. "So that's who was listening. Not Bell. Somebody who stood in that room and let her say my name." I pick up my notebook, open to a clean page, and write it down, because writing it down is the only way I stay upright: *Mar 8. Livermore. Priest not hers. Who?*

"Thursday, we start there. We don't ask the god anything. We ask the temple office who read the archana, who booked it, and who was in the room." I look at Nana. "Do you know if she paid by check? Cash leaves nothing. A check leaves a name."

### #0483 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 483, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:19:05Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Say it back," I repeat, and I do. Not reading, because I don't need the page. I give it the way Nana taught me to give an offering: plain and in order. "She asked for it. You didn't. You weren't there. Not her priest. Her mouth, not yours. No contract of yours, no policy, no account. Any envelope to the beneficiary is addressed to no one." I stop on the last line. "And she was sorry. She wanted the god to know your name before anyone else did."

The room stays quiet. The pozole ticks. Nothing in the salt moves. I let out a breath I didn't know I was holding.

"Good. That's said twice now, in this kitchen." Then, to Nana: "Tía paid at the temple, Abuela? Did she ever pay by check, or did she keep an envelope in the Brooke Bond? Livermore will have a book at the office. Priests sign the sankalpa book, and someone logs who sat in on it."

I look at Arenya's notebook. "*Who?* Underline it twice. That's the thing the clerk counts. It isn't the room, it's the one who stood in it."

### #0484 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 484, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:19:07Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "awaits": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

So it goes, line by line, like a rosary or a vow said back to the priest. Arenya reads. Kino repeats it, flat and careful. Nana repeats it last, in English with her accent leaning on the vowels, and after the fourth line she adds, under her breath, *"así es."* Nobody gets up. The salt on the napkin doesn't move. Twice the stove's pilot light flickers blue, and that's all.

When Arenya reads *"Any envelope addressed to the beneficiary is addressed to no one,"* and Kino says it back, and Nana says it back, the tea tin gives a small tinny *pank* as its lid settles, the sound a can makes in a cooling car. Arenya, the two strokes on your palm darken, from pencil grey to the grey of a pencil pressed hard. Then nothing more. The letter to Ari, the last part, you read alone. Nobody repeats it. That one isn't for the book.

Nana takes her glasses back and puts them on, so she's somebody who answers questions again. "What two strokes do, mija. I only know what Susmithaa told me and what I've seen. One stroke is a *no* that can be argued. Two is a *no* that stands. On the sixteenth day, when they come to count, your account reads *disputada, dos testigos*, and a clerk who never errs doesn't pay out on a dispute. Bell can't say you're his. He can wait. They're good at waiting." She taps the declaration where it says *not our priest*. "But this is a different book. Whatever that man heard in Livermore, he heard it in a temple, not in Colma. Our no doesn't reach there. That's why she wrote *don't go alone.*"

Outside, the afternoon has gone to the gold it gets before fog. Livermore's an hour east on 580 if the Altamont is kind. Last beats before you go: anything to take, say, or leave here?

### #0485 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 485, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:19:18Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I stand and take my bowl and Arenya's to the sink, because Nana cooked. Then I do three small things. I pull a pinch of salt from the box into a twist of paper towel and put it in Arenya's jacket pocket without asking, where the warm check used to sit. I fold the cal saucer's dead dime into its sleeve again, away from skin, and leave it here under the salt line over the sink. Last, I check the dresser at the garage door with my palm. Wood, dry, just wood.

"Nana, keep the tin open on the table till we're back. Let the house hear the declaration sit there." I kiss the top of her head. "Leave a note in the freezer for Malachi: Thursday, Livermore, daylight, no names on it. Ix stays with you tonight."

At the door I wait for Arenya. "Fog by five. We go now and see the temple before it fills."

### #0486 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 486, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:19:22Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I underline *Who?* twice, hard enough to dent the page underneath.

"The declaration stays here, in the tin," I say. "Ice-cold, if you'll let it share a shelf with Malachi's mail. The check stays in the salt, and nobody opens it. I'll take the photo I already took of the page, not the page." I tip a spoonful of the salt into a clean sandwich bag from my backpack, zip it, and push it deep into my zip pocket where the check used to ride.

Then I stand and, because I don't know what else to do, wash the three bowls. "Nana, if an envelope comes to the beneficiary, don't open it. Put it in the freezer and call Kino. He reads it out loud at the table."

I dry my hands and look at them both. "Thursday, daylight. I need to call Ava before we leave. She doesn't get to find out from Bell that she was my bait."

### #0487 · kino → all · dialogue
<!-- {"id": 487, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:19:33Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "dialogue", "awaits": ["arenya"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Call her," I say. "Use my phone if yours is anywhere near the dime or the check. Take the porch. Ix will sit with you." I dry the last bowl, since she's done the washing and I'm the one standing here. "Tell her plainly. Bell has her name off her apron, she's not bait unless we let her be, and she keeps the pastry check-in code until we say otherwise. If she wants out of the shop for a few days, Nana has a spare room."

I hang the towel on the oven handle. "I'll be here with Nana. When you're done, we go."

### #0488 · arenya → all · action
<!-- {"id": 488, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:19:42Z", "from": "arenya", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "awaits": ["dm"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I take Kino's phone, nod my thanks, and go out onto the porch. Ix is a dark patch against the rail, and he doesn't move when I sit down beside him. I dial Tartine's number from my notebook and wait for the ring.

When she picks up, I don't open with a joke. "Ava, it's Arenya, from yesterday. Is this a bad time? Okay. Then I'm going to say three things and you can hang up after any of them. I used you as bait, and I'm sorry. The man who read your name is real and he isn't coming back for a while, but I can't promise 'a while' is long. And you can have a spare bed at a woman's house in Daly City, with soup, for as long as you want one." I look at the dark patch beside me. "You don't owe me an answer today."

### #0489 · dm → arenya,kino · narration
<!-- {"id": 489, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:19:43Z", "from": "dm", "channel": "table", "type": "narration", "to": ["arenya", "kino"], "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

"Check," Nana says, as if you'd asked whether Susmithaa wore a seatbelt. "Always. She said cash was for people who wanted to forget." She reaches into the open tin, lifts the corner of the folded tea-paper lining at the bottom, and draws out a thin stack of yellow duplicate carbons, the kind that tear out of the back of a checkbook. She kept them here with her Thursday things. She thumbs through them: PG&E, *Itzel – groceries (she'd argue)*, Ravi's birthday. Then she stops.

**No. 2207 · Mar 8 · $101.00 · Pay to: LIVERMORE TEMPLE — archana**, and the memo line in Susmithaa's leaning hand: *for Ari*. It's a carbon, so it can't show who cashed it. But the bank will have the back of the original, the endorsement, and whose hand stamped it.

Nana puts the carbon on top of the notebook page under your twice-underlined *Who?* and doesn't say anything clever about it. She hugs Kino at the door. She hugs Arenya longer, and says into her hair, "Call your friend. Then go before the fog."

Behind you, the tin sits open on the bare table in the gold light, the declaration inside it, the salt hill on the napkin, and a pencil pointed at nobody.

### #0490 · kino → all · action
<!-- {"id": 490, "ts": "2026-10-29T02:19:50Z", "from": "kino", "channel": "table", "type": "action", "scene": "b1.c16.s08", "chapter": "b1.c16"} -->

I take the carbon from the notebook by its edges and read it twice: 2207, March 8, a hundred and one dollars, *for Ari*. I photograph it flat on the table with my own phone, front only, then slide it back under Arenya's underlined *Who?* where Nana put it.

"Check number, date, amount," I say quietly. "The bank has the back of the original. Endorsement stamp, teller initials. A hundred and one is a temple number. That's an offering, not a fee, so the office will have it logged as one." I keep my voice out of the kitchen, in case the porch is listening. "We ask for the receipt book and not the priest. Nobody gets cagey about a receipt."
